<sup>33</sup> Jehovah will not leave him in his hand,<br>Nor condemn him when he is judged.<br>
<sup>34</sup> Wait for Jehovah, and keep his way,<br>And he will exalt thee to inherit the land:<br>When the wicked are cut off, thou shalt see it.<br>
<sup>35</sup> I have seen the wicked in great power,<br>And spreading himself like a green tree in its native soil.<br>
<sup>36</sup> <sup>l</sup>But one passed by, and, lo, he was not:<br>Yea, I sought him, but he could not be found.<br>
<sup>37</sup> Mark the perfect man, and behold the upright;<br>For there is a _happy_ end to the man of peace.<br>
<sup>38</sup> As for transgressors, they shall be destroyed together:<br>The end of the wicked shall be cut off.<br>
<sup>39</sup> But the salvation of the righteous is of Jehovah:<br>He is their stronghold in the time of trouble.<br>
<sup>40</sup> And Jehovah helpeth them, and rescueth them:<br>He rescueth them from the wicked, and saveth them,<br>Because they have taken refuge in him.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 3. Or, _So shalt thou dwell in the land and feed securely_
> - <sup>b</sup> 3. Or, verily _thou shalt be fed_
> - <sup>c</sup> 4. Or, _So shalt thou have thy delight in etc._
> - <sup>d</sup> 4. Hebrew _petitions_.
> - <sup>e</sup> 5. Hebrew _Roll thy way upon Jehovah_.
> - <sup>f</sup> 7. Or, _Be still before_ (Hebrew _silent to_) _Jehovah_
> - <sup>g</sup> 9. Or, _the earth_ (and so in verses 11, 22, 29, 34)
> - <sup>h</sup> 10. Or, _it_
> - <sup>i</sup> 20. Or, _the excellency of the pastures_
> - <sup>j</sup> 20. Or, _like smoke_
> - <sup>k</sup> 24. Or, _upholdeth his hand_
> - <sup>l</sup> 36. Or, _Yet he passed away_

## Chapter 38

### Prayer of a suffering penitent.

<sup>1</sup> > _A Psalm of David, to bring to remembrance._O Jehovah, rebuke me not in thy wrath;<br>Neither cha<sup>a</sup>sten me in thy hot displeasure.<br>
<sup>2</sup> For thine arrows <sup>b</sup>stick fast in me,<br>And thy hand <sup>c</sup>presseth me sore.<br>
<sup>3</sup> There is no soundness in my flesh because of thine indignation;<br>Neither is there any <sup>d</sup>health in my bones because of my sin.<br>
<sup>4</sup> For mine iniquities are gone over my head:<br>As a heavy burden they are too heavy for me.<br>
<sup>5</sup> My wounds are loathsome and corrupt,<br>Because of my foolishness.<br>
<sup>6</sup> I am <sup>e</sup>pained and bowed down greatly;<br>I go mourning all the day long.<br>
<sup>7</sup> For my loins are filled with burning;<br>And there is no soundness in my flesh.<br>
<sup>8</sup> I am faint and sore bruised:<br>I have <sup>f</sup>groaned by reason of the disquietness of my heart.<br>
<sup>9</sup> Lord, all my desire is before thee;<br>And my groaning is not hid from thee.<br>
<sup>10</sup> My heart throbbeth, my strength faileth me:<br>As for the light of mine eyes, it also is gone from me.<br>
<sup>11</sup> My lovers and my friends stand aloof from my plague;<br>And my kinsmen stand afar off.<br>
<sup>12</sup> They also that seek after my life lay snares _for me_;<br>And they that seek my hurt speak mischievous things,<br>And meditate deceits all the day long.<br>
<sup>13</sup> But I, as a deaf man, hear not;<br>And I am as a dumb man that openeth not his mouth.<br>
<sup>14</sup> Yea, I am as a man that heareth not,<br>And in whose mouth are no <sup>g</sup>reproofs.<br>
<sup>15</sup> For in thee, O Jehovah, do I hope:<br>Thou wilt answer, O Lord my God.<br>
<sup>16</sup> For I said, Lest they rejoice over me:<br>When my foot slippeth, they magnify themselves against me.<br>
<sup>17</sup> For I am ready to fall,<br>And my sorrow is continually before me.<br>
<sup>18</sup> For I will declare mine iniquity;<br>I will be sorry for my sin.<br>
<sup>19</sup> But mine enemies are lively, _and_ are strong;<br>And they that hate me <sup>h</sup>wrongfully are multiplied.<br>
<sup>20</sup> They also that render evil for good<br>Are adversaries unto me, because I follow the thing that is good.<br>
<sup>21</sup> Forsake me not, O Jehovah:<br>O my God, be not far from me.<br>
<sup>22</sup> Make haste to help me,<br>O Lord, my salvation.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 1. Or, _to make memorial_
> - <sup>b</sup> 2. Hebrew _lighted on me_.
> - <sup>c</sup> 2. Hebrew _lighted on me_.
> - <sup>d</sup> 3. Or, _rest_
> - <sup>e</sup> 6. Hebrew _bent_.
> - <sup>f</sup> 8. Hebrew _roared_.
> - <sup>g</sup> 14. Or, _arguments_
> - <sup>h</sup> 19. Hebrew _falsely_.

## Chapter 39

### The vanity of life.

<sup>1</sup> > _For the Chief Musician, for Jeduthun. A Psalm of David._I said, I will take heed to my ways,<br>That I sin not <sup>a</sup>with my tongue:<br>I will keep my mouth with a bridle,<br>While the wicked is before me.<sup>b</sup><br>
<sup>2</sup> I was dumb with silence, I held my peace, <sup>c</sup>even from good;<br>And my sorrow was stirred.<br>
<sup>3</sup> My heart was hot within me;<br>While I was musing the fire burned;<br>_Then_ spake I with my tongue:<br>
<sup>4</sup> Jehovah, make me to know mine end,<br>And the measure of my days, what it is;<br>Let me know how frail I am.<br>
<sup>5</sup> Behold, thou hast made my days _as_ handbreadths;<br>And my life-time is as nothing before thee:<br>Surely every man <sup>d</sup>at his best estate is altogether <sup>e</sup>vanity. _[Selah_<br>
<sup>6</sup> Surely every man walketh <sup>f</sup>in a vain show;<br>Surely they are disquieted <sup>g</sup>in vain:<br>He heapeth up _riches_, and knoweth not who shall gather them.<br>
<sup>7</sup> And now, Lord, what wait I for?<br>My hope is in thee.<br>
<sup>8</sup> Deliver me from all my transgressions:<br>Make me not the reproach of the foolish.<br>
<sup>9</sup> I was dumb, I opened not my mouth;<br>Because thou didst it.<br>
<sup>10</sup> Remove thy stroke away from me:<br>I am consumed by the <sup>h</sup>blow of thy hand.<br>
<sup>11</sup> When thou with rebukes dost correct man for iniquity,<br>Thou <sup>i</sup>makest his beauty to consume away like a moth:<br>Surely every man is <sup>j</sup>vanity. _[Selah_<br>
<sup>12</sup> Hear my prayer, O Jehovah, and give ear unto my cry;<br>Hold not thy peace at my tears:<br>For I am a stranger with thee,<br>A sojourner, as all my fathers were.<br>
<sup>13</sup> Oh <sup>k</sup>spare me, that I may <sup>l</sup>recover strength,<br>Before I go hence, and be no more.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 1. 62:title; 77:title; 1 Chr. 16:41; 25:1.
> - <sup>b</sup> 1. Hebrew _a bridle_ (or _muzzle_) _for my mouth_.
> - <sup>c</sup> 2. Or, _and had no comfort_. Hebrew _away from good_.
> - <sup>d</sup> 5. Hebrew _standing firm_.
> - <sup>e</sup> 5. Hebrew _a breath_.
> - <sup>f</sup> 6. Or, _as a shadow_
> - <sup>g</sup> 6. Or, _for vanity_
> - <sup>h</sup> 10. Hebrew _conflict_.
> - <sup>i</sup> 11. Or, _consumest like a moth his delights_
> - <sup>j</sup> 11. Hebrew _a breath_.
> - <sup>k</sup> 13. Or, _look away from me_
> - <sup>l</sup> 13. Hebrew _brighten up_.

## Chapter 40

### A sacrifice of praise, and prayer for help.

<sup>1</sup> > _For the Chief Musician. A Psalm of David._I waited patiently for Jehovah;<br>And he inclined unto me, and heard my cry.<br>
<sup>2</sup> He brought me up also out of <sup>a</sup>a horrible pit, out of the miry clay;<br>And he set my feet upon a rock, and established my goings.<br>
<sup>3</sup> And he hath put a new song in my mouth, even praise unto our God:<br>Many shall see it, and fear,<br>And shall trust in Jehovah.<br>
<sup>4</sup> Blessed is the man that maketh Jehovah his trust,<br>And respecteth not the proud, nor such as <sup>b</sup>turn aside to lies.<br>
<sup>5</sup> Many, O Jehovah my God, are the wonderful works which thou hast done,<br>And thy thoughts which are to us-ward:<sup>c</sup><br>They cannot be set in order unto thee;<br>If I would declare and speak of them,<br>They are more than can be numbered.<br>
<sup>6</sup> Sacrifice and <sup>d</sup>offering thou hast no delight in;<sup>e</sup><br>Mine ears hast thou opened:<br>Burnt-offering and sin-offering hast thou not required.<br>
<sup>7</sup> Then said I, Lo, I am come;<br>In the roll of the book it is <sup>f</sup>written of me:<br>
<sup>8</sup> I delight to do thy will, O my God;<br>Yea, thy law is within my heart.<br>
<sup>9</sup> I have proclaimed glad tidings of righteousness in the great assembly;<br>Lo, I will not refrain my lips,<br>O Jehovah, thou knowest.<br>
<sup>10</sup> I have not hid thy righteousness within my heart;<br>I have declared thy faithfulness and thy salvation;<br>I have not concealed thy lovingkindness and thy truth from the great assembly.<br>
<sup>11</sup> Withhold not thou thy tender mercies from me, O Jehovah;<br>Let thy lovingkindness and thy truth continually preserve me.<br>
<sup>12</sup> For innumerable evils have compassed me about;<br>Mine iniquities have overtaken me, so that I am not able to look up;<br>They are more than the hairs of my head;<br>And my heart hath <sup>g</sup>failed me.<br>
<sup>13</sup> Be pleased, O Jehovah, to deliver me:<br>Make haste to help me, O Jehovah.<br>
<sup>14</sup> Let them be put to shame and confounded together<br>That seek after my soul to destroy it:<br>Let them be turned backward and brought to dishonor<br>That delight in my hurt.<br>
<sup>15</sup> Let them be <sup>h</sup>desolate <sup>i</sup>by reason of their shame<br>That say unto me, Aha, aha.<br>
<sup>16</sup> Let all those that seek thee rejoice and be glad in thee:<br>Let such as love thy salvation say continually,<br>Jehovah be magnified.<br>
<sup>17</sup> But I am poor and needy;<br>_Yet_ the Lord thinketh upon me:<br>Thou art my help and my deliverer;<br>Make no tarrying, O my God.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 2. Hebrew _a pit of tumult_ (or _destruction_).
> - <sup>b</sup> 4. Or, _fall away treacherously_
> - <sup>c</sup> 5. Or, _There is none to be compared unto thee_
> - <sup>d</sup> 6. Or, _meal-offering_
> - <sup>e</sup> 6. Hebrew _Ears hast thou digged_ (or, _pierced_) _for me_.
> - <sup>f</sup> 7. Or, _prescribed to_
> - <sup>g</sup> 12. Hebrew _forsaken_.
> - <sup>h</sup> 15. Or, _astonished_
> - <sup>i</sup> 15. Or, _for a reward of_

## Chapter 41

### The psalmist in sickness complains of enemies and false friends.

<sup>1</sup> > _For the Chief Musician. A Psalm of David._Blessed is he that considereth the poor:<br>Jehovah will deliver him in the day of evil.<sup>a</sup><br>
<sup>2</sup> Jehovah will preserve him, and keep him alive,<br>And he shall be blessed <sup>b</sup>upon the earth;<br>And deliver not thou him unto the will of his enemies.<br>
<sup>3</sup> Jehovah will support him upon the couch of languishing:<br>Thou <sup>c</sup>makest all his bed in his sickness.<br>
<sup>4</sup> I said, O Jehovah, have mercy upon me:<br>Heal my soul; for I have sinned against thee.<br>
<sup>5</sup> Mine enemies speak evil against me, _saying_,<br>When will he die, and his name perish?<br>
<sup>6</sup> And if he come to see _me_, he speaketh falsehood;<br>His heart gathereth iniquity to itself:<br>When he goeth abroad, he telleth it.<br>
<sup>7</sup> All that hate me whisper together against me;<br>Against me do they devise my hurt.<br>
<sup>8</sup> <sup>d</sup>An evil disease, _say they_, <sup>e</sup>cleaveth fast unto him;<br>And now that he lieth he shall rise up no more.<br>
<sup>9</sup> Yea, mine own familiar friend, in whom I trusted,<br>Who did eat of my bread,<br>Hath lifted up his heel against me.<br>
<sup>10</sup> But thou, O Jehovah, have mercy upon me, and raise me up,<br>That I may requite them.<br>
<sup>11</sup> By this I know that thou delightest in me,<br>Because mine enemy doth not triumph over me.<br>
<sup>12</sup> And as for me, thou upholdest me in mine integrity,<br>And settest me before thy face for ever.<br>
<sup>13</sup> Blessed be Jehovah, the God of Israel,<br>From everlasting and to everlasting.<br>Amen, and Amen.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 1. Or, _the weak_
> - <sup>b</sup> 2. Or, _in the land_
> - <sup>c</sup> 3. Hebrew _turnest_, or, _changest_.
> - <sup>d</sup> 8. Or, _Some wicked thing_
> - <sup>e</sup> 8. Or, _is poured out upon him_

## Chapter 42

### Thirsting for God in trouble and exile.

<sup>1</sup> > _For the Chief Musician. Maschil of the sons of Korah._As the hart panteth after the water brooks,<br>So panteth my soul after thee, O God.<br>
<sup>2</sup> My soul thirsteth for God, for the living God:<br>When shall I come and appear before God?<br>
<sup>3</sup> My tears have been my food day and night,<br>While they <sup>a</sup>continually say unto me, Where is thy God?<br>
<sup>4</sup> These things I remember, and pour out my soul <sup>b</sup>within me,<br>How I went with the throng, and <sup>c</sup>led them to the house of God,<br>With the voice of joy and praise, a multitude keeping holyday.<br>
<sup>5</sup> Why art thou <sup>d</sup>cast down, O my soul?<br>And _why_ art thou disquieted within me?<br>Hope thou in God; for I shall yet praise him<br>_For_ the help of his countenance.<br>
<sup>6</sup> O my God, my soul is cast down within me:<br>Therefore do I remember thee from the land of the Jordan,<br>And the Hermons, from <sup>e</sup>the hill Mizar.<br>
<sup>7</sup> Deep calleth unto deep at the noise of thy waterfalls:<br>All thy waves and thy billows are gone over me.<br>
<sup>8</sup> _Yet_ Jehovah will command his lovingkindness in the day-time;<br>And in the night his song shall be with me,<br>_Even_ a prayer unto the God of my life.<br>
<sup>9</sup> I will say unto God my rock, Why hast thou forgotten me?<br>Why go I mourning <sup>f</sup>because of the oppression of the enemy?<br>
<sup>10</sup> As with <sup>g</sup>a sword in my bones, mine adversaries reproach me,<br>While they continually say unto me, Where is thy God?<br>
<sup>11</sup> Why art thou cast down, O my soul?<br>And why art thou disquieted within me?<br>Hope thou in God; for I shall yet praise him,<br>_Who is_ the help of my countenance, and my God.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 3. Hebrew _all the day_.
> - <sup>b</sup> 4. Hebrew _upon_.
> - <sup>c</sup> 4. Or, _went in procession with them_
> - <sup>d</sup> 5. Hebrew _bowed down_.
> - <sup>e</sup> 6. Or, _the little mountain_
> - <sup>f</sup> 9. Or, _while the enemy oppresseth_
> - <sup>g</sup> 10. Or, _crushing_

## Chapter 43

### Prayer for deliverance.

<sup>1</sup> Judge me, O God, and plead my cause against an ungodly nation:<br>Oh deliver me from the deceitful and unjust man.<br>
<sup>2</sup> For thou art the God of my strength; why hast thou cast me off?<br>Why go I mourning <sup>a</sup>because of the oppression of the enemy?<br>
<sup>3</sup> Oh send out thy light and thy truth; let them lead me:<br>Let them bring me unto thy holy hill,<br>And to thy tabernacles.<br>
<sup>4</sup> Then will I go unto the altar of God,<br>Unto God <sup>b</sup>my exceeding joy;<br>And upon the harp will I praise thee, O God, my God.<br>
<sup>5</sup> Why art thou cast down, O my soul?<br>And why art thou disquieted within me?<br>Hope thou in God; for I shall yet praise him,<br>_Who is_ the help of my countenance, and my God.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 2. Or, _while the enemy oppresseth_
> - <sup>b</sup> 4. Hebrew _the gladness of my joy_.

## Chapter 44

### Former deliverances and present troubles.

<sup>1</sup> > _For the Chief Musician. _A Psalm_ of the sons of Korah. Maschil._We have heard with our ears, O God,<br>Our fathers have told us,<br>What work thou didst in their days,<br>In the days of old.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Thou didst drive out the nations with thy hand;<br>But them thou didst plant:<br>Thou didst afflict the peoples;<br>But them thou didst spread abroad.<br>
<sup>3</sup> For they gat not the land in possession by their own sword,<br>Neither did their own arm save them;<br>But thy right hand, and thine arm, and the light of thy countenance,<br>Because thou wast favorable unto them.<br>
<sup>4</sup> Thou art my King, O God:<br>Command <sup>a</sup>deliverance for Jacob.<br>
<sup>5</sup> Through thee will we push down our adversaries:<br>Through thy name will we tread them under that rise up against us.<br>
<sup>6</sup> For I will not trust in my bow,<br>Neither shall my sword save me.<br>
<sup>7</sup> But thou hast saved us from our adversaries,<br>And hast put them to shame that hate us.<br>
<sup>8</sup> In God have we made our boast all the day long,<br>And we will give thanks unto thy name for ever. _[Selah_<br>
<sup>9</sup> But now thou hast cast _us_ off, and brought us to dishonor,<br>And goest not forth with our hosts.<br>
<sup>10</sup> Thou makest us to turn back from the adversary;<br>And they that hate us take spoil for themselves.<br>
<sup>11</sup> Thou hast made us like sheep _appointed_ for food,<br>And hast scattered us among the nations.<br>
<sup>12</sup> Thou sellest thy people for nought,<br>And hast not increased _thy wealth_ by their price.<br>
<sup>13</sup> Thou makest us a reproach to our neighbors,<br>A scoffing and a derision to them that are round about us.<br>
<sup>14</sup> Thou makest us a byword among the nations,<br>A shaking of the head among the peoples.<br>
<sup>15</sup> All the day long is my dishonor before me,<br>And the shame of my face hath covered me,<br>
<sup>16</sup> For the voice of him that reproacheth and blasphemeth,<br>By reason of the enemy and the avenger.<br>
<sup>17</sup> All this is come upon us; yet have we not forgotten thee,<br>Neither have we dealt falsely in thy covenant.<br>
<sup>18</sup> Our heart is not turned back,<br>Neither have our steps declined from thy way,<br>
<sup>19</sup> <sup>b</sup>That thou hast sore broken us in the place of jackals,<br>And covered us with the shadow of death.<br>
<sup>20</sup> If we have forgotten the name of our God,<br>Or spread forth our hands to a strange god;<br>
<sup>21</sup> Will not God search this out?<br>For he knoweth the secrets of the heart.<br>
<sup>22</sup> Yea, for thy sake are we killed all the day long;<br>We are accounted as sheep for the slaughter.<br>
<sup>23</sup> Awake, why sleepest thou, O Lord?<br>Arise, cast _us_ not off for ever.<br>
<sup>24</sup> Wherefore hidest thou thy face,<br>And forgettest our affliction and our oppression?<br>
<sup>25</sup> For our soul is bowed down to the dust:<br>Our body cleaveth unto the earth.<br>
<sup>26</sup> Rise up for our help,<br>And redeem us for thy lovingkindness’ sake.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 4. Or, _victories_
> - <sup>b</sup> 19. Or, _Though_

## Chapter 45

### A song celebrating the king’s marriage.

<sup>1</sup> > _For the Chief Musician; set to Shoshannim. _A Psalm_ of the sons of Korah. Maschil. A Song of loves._My heart overfloweth with a goodly matter;<br>I speak the things which I ha<sup>a</sup>ve made touching the king:<br>My tongue is the pen of a ready writer.<sup>b</sup><br>
<sup>2</sup> Thou art fairer than the children of men;<br>Grace is poured <sup>c</sup>into thy lips:<br>Therefore God hath blessed thee for ever.<br>
<sup>3</sup> Gird thy sword upon thy thigh, O mighty one,<br>Thy glory and thy majesty.<br>
<sup>4</sup> And in thy majesty ride on prosperously,<sup>d</sup><br>Because of truth and meekness _and_ righteousness:<br>And <sup>e</sup>thy right hand shall teach thee terrible things.<br>
<sup>5</sup> Thine arrows are sharp;<br>The peoples fall under thee;<br>_They are_ in the heart of the king’s enemies.<br>
<sup>6</sup> <sup>f</sup>Thy throne, O God, is for ever and ever:<br>A sceptre of equity is the sceptre of thy kingdom.<br>
<sup>7</sup> Thou hast loved righteousness, and hated wickedness:<br>Therefore God, thy God, hath anointed thee<br>With the oil of gladness above thy fellows.<br>
<sup>8</sup> All thy garments _smell of_ myrrh, and aloes, _and_ cassia;<br>Out of ivory palaces stringed instruments have made thee glad.<br>
<sup>9</sup> Kings’ daughters are among thy honorable women:<br>At thy right hand doth stand the queen in gold of Ophir.<br>
<sup>10</sup> Hearken, O daughter, and consider, and incline thine ear;<br>Forget also thine own people, and thy father’s house:<br>
<sup>11</sup> So will the king desire thy beauty;<br>For he is thy lord; and reverence thou him.<br>
<sup>12</sup> And the daughter of Tyre _shall be there_ with a gift;<br>The rich among the people shall entreat thy favor.<br>
<sup>13</sup> The king’s daughter <sup>g</sup>within _the palace_ is all glorious:<br>Her clothing is inwrought with gold.<br>
<sup>14</sup> She shall be led unto the king <sup>h</sup>in broidered work:<br>The virgins her companions that follow her<br>Shall be brought unto thee.<br>
<sup>15</sup> With gladness and rejoicing shall they be led:<br>They shall enter into the king’s palace.<br>
<sup>16</sup> Instead of thy fathers shall be thy children,<br>Whom thou shalt make princes in all the earth.<br>
<sup>17</sup> I will make thy name to be remembered in all generations:<br>Therefore shall the peoples give thee thanks for ever and ever.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 1. That is, _Lilies_.
> - <sup>b</sup> 1. Or, _I speak: my work is for a king_
> - <sup>c</sup> 2. Or, _upon_
> - <sup>d</sup> 4. Or, _In behalf of_
> - <sup>e</sup> 4. Or, _let thy right hand teach_
> - <sup>f</sup> 6. Or, _Thy throne is_ the throne of _God etc._
> - <sup>g</sup> 13. Or, _in the inner part_ of the palace
> - <sup>h</sup> 14. Or, _upon_

## Chapter 46

### God the refuge of his people.

<sup>1</sup> > _For the Chief Musician. _A Psalm_ of the sons of Korah; set to Alamoth. A Song._God is our refuge and strength,<br>A very present help in trouble.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Therefore will we not fear, though the earth do change,<br>And though the mountains be shaken into the heart of the seas;<br>
<sup>3</sup> Though the waters thereof roar and be troubled,<br>Though the mountains tremble with the <sup>a</sup>swelling thereof. _[Selah_<br>
<sup>4</sup> There is a river, the streams whereof make glad the city of God,<br>The holy place of the tabernacles of the Most High.<br>
<sup>5</sup> God is in the midst of her; she shall not be moved:<br>God will help her, <sup>b</sup>and that right early.<br>
<sup>6</sup> The nations raged, the kingdoms were moved:<br>He uttered his voice, the earth melted.<br>
<sup>7</sup> Jehovah of hosts is with us;<br>The God of Jacob is our <sup>c</sup>refuge. _[Selah_<br>
<sup>8</sup> Come, behold the works of Jehovah,<sup>d</sup><br>What desolations he hath made in the earth.<br>
<sup>9</sup> He maketh wars to cease unto the end of the earth;<br>He breaketh the bow, and cutteth the spear in sunder;<br>He burneth the chariots in the fire.<br>
<sup>10</sup> <sup>e</sup>Be still, and know that I am God:<br>I will be exalted among the nations, I will be exalted in the earth.<br>
<sup>11</sup> Jehovah of hosts is with us;<br>The God of Jacob is our <sup>f</sup>refuge. _[Selah_<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 3. Or, _pride_
> - <sup>b</sup> 5. Hebrew _at the dawn of morning_.
> - <sup>c</sup> 7. Or, _high tower_
> - <sup>d</sup> 8. Or, _Who hath made desolation etc._
> - <sup>e</sup> 10. Or, _Let be_
> - <sup>f</sup> 11. Or, _high tower_

## Chapter 47

### God the King of the Earth.

<sup>1</sup> > _For the Chief Musician. A Psalm of the sons of Korah._Oh clap your hands, all ye peoples;<br>Shout unto God with the voice of triumph.<br>
<sup>2</sup> For <sup>a</sup>Jehovah Most High is terrible;<br>He is a great King over all the earth.<br>
<sup>3</sup> He subdueth peoples under us,<br>And nations under our feet.<br>
<sup>4</sup> He chooseth our inheritance for us,<br>The glory of Jacob whom he <sup>b</sup>loved. _[Selah_<br>
<sup>5</sup> God is gone up with a shout,<br>Jehovah with the sound of a trumpet.<br>
<sup>6</sup> Sing praises to God, sing praises:<br>Sing praises unto our King, sing praises.<br>
<sup>7</sup> For God is the King of all the earth:<br>Sing ye praises <sup>c</sup>with understanding.<br>
<sup>8</sup> God reigneth over the nations:<br>God sitteth upon his holy throne.<br>
<sup>9</sup> The princes of the peoples are gathered together<sup>d</sup><br>_To be_ the people of the God of Abraham;<br>For the shields of the earth belong unto God:<br>He is greatly exalted.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 2. Or, _Jehovah is most high_ and _terrible_
> - <sup>b</sup> 4. Or, _loveth_
> - <sup>c</sup> 7. Or, _in a skilful psalm_. Hebrew _Maschil_.
> - <sup>d</sup> 9. Or, Unto _the people_

## Chapter 48

### The beauty and glory of Zion.

<sup>1</sup> > _A Song; a Psalm of the sons of Korah._Great is Jehovah, and greatly to be praised,<br>In the city of our God, in his holy mountain.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Beautiful in elevation, the joy of the whole earth,<br>Is mount Zion, _on_ the sides of the north,<br>The city of the great King.<br>
<sup>3</sup> God hath made himself known in her palaces for a <sup>a</sup>refuge.<br>
<sup>4</sup> For, lo, the kings assembled themselves,<br>They <sup>b</sup>passed by together.<br>
<sup>5</sup> They saw it, then were they amazed;<br>They were dismayed, they <sup>c</sup>hasted away.<br>
<sup>6</sup> Trembling took hold of them there,<br>Pain, as of a woman in travail.<br>
<sup>7</sup> <sup>d</sup>With the east wind<br>Thou breakest the ships of Tarshish.<br>
<sup>8</sup> As we have heard, so have we seen<br>In the city of Jehovah of hosts, in the city of our God:<br>God will establish it for ever. _[Selah_<br>
<sup>9</sup> We have thought on thy lovingkindness, O God,<br>In the midst of thy temple.<br>
<sup>10</sup> As is thy name, O God,<br>So is thy praise unto the ends of the earth:<br>Thy right hand is full of righteousness.<br>
<sup>11</sup> Let mount Zion be glad,<br>Let the daughters of Judah rejoice,<br>Because of thy judgments.<br>
<sup>12</sup> Walk about Zion, and go round about her;<br>Number the towers thereof;<br>
<sup>13</sup> Mark ye well her bulwarks;<sup>e</sup><br>Consider her palaces:<br>That ye may tell it to the generation following.<br>
<sup>14</sup> For this God is our God for ever and ever:<br>He will be our guide _even_ unto death.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 3. Or, _high tower_
> - <sup>b</sup> 4. Or, _passed away_
> - <sup>c</sup> 5. Or, _were stricken with terror_
> - <sup>d</sup> 7. Or, As _with the east wind that breaketh_
> - <sup>e</sup> 13. Or, _Traverse_

## Chapter 49

### The folly of trusting in riches.

<sup>1</sup> > _For the Chief Musician. A Psalm of the sons of Korah._Hear this, all ye peoples;<br>Give ear, all ye inhabitants of the world,<br>
<sup>2</sup> Both low and high,<br>Rich and poor together.<br>
<sup>3</sup> My mouth shall speak wisdom;<br>And the meditation of my heart shall be of understanding.<br>
<sup>4</sup> I will incline mine ear to a parable:<br>I will open my dark saying upon the harp.<br>
<sup>5</sup> Wherefore should I fear in the days of evil,<br>When <sup>a</sup>iniquity at my heels compasseth me about?<br>
<sup>6</sup> They that trust in their wealth,<br>And boast themselves in the multitude of their riches;<br>
<sup>7</sup> None _of them_ can by any means redeem his brother,<br>Nor give to God a ransom for him<br>
<sup>8</sup> (For the redemption of their life is costly,<br>And it faileth for ever),<br>
<sup>9</sup> That he should still live alway,<br>That he should not see <sup>b</sup>corruption.<br>
<sup>10</sup> For <sup>c</sup>he shall see it. Wise men die;<br>The fool and the brutish alike perish,<br>And leave their wealth to others.<br>
<sup>11</sup> Their inward thought is, _that_ their houses _shall continue_ for ever,<br>_And_ their dwelling-places to all generations;<br>They call their lands after their own names.<br>
<sup>12</sup> But man _being_ in honor abideth not:<br>He is like the beasts that perish.<br>
<sup>13</sup> This <sup>d</sup>their way is <sup>e</sup>their folly:<br>Yet after them men approve their sayings. _[Selah_<br>
<sup>14</sup> They are appointed as a flock for Sheol;<br>Death shall be their shepherd:<br>And the upright shall have dominion over them in the morning;<br>And their <sup>f</sup>beauty shall be for Sheol to consume,<br>That there be no habitation for it.<br>
<sup>15</sup> But God will redeem my soul from the <sup>g</sup>power of Sheol;<br>For he will receive me. _[Selah_<br>
<sup>16</sup> Be not thou afraid when one is made rich,<br>When the <sup>h</sup>glory of his house is increased:<br>
<sup>17</sup> For when he dieth he shall carry nothing away;<br>His glory shall not descend after him.<br>
<sup>18</sup> Though while he lived he blessed his soul<br>(And men praise thee, when thou doest well to thyself),<br>
<sup>19</sup> <sup>i</sup>He shall go to the generation of his fathers;<sup>j</sup><br>They shall never see the light.<br>
<sup>20</sup> Man that is in honor, and understandeth not,<br>Is like the beasts that perish.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 5. Or, _the iniquity of them that would supplant me compasseth me about, Even of them that trust . . . riches?_
> - <sup>b</sup> 9. Or, _the pit_
> - <sup>c</sup> 10. Or, _he seeth that wise men etc._
> - <sup>d</sup> 13. Or, _is the way of them that are foolish_
> - <sup>e</sup> 13. Or, _their confidence: and after etc._
> - <sup>f</sup> 14. Or, _form_
> - <sup>g</sup> 15. Hebrew _hand_.
> - <sup>h</sup> 16. Or, _wealth_
> - <sup>i</sup> 19. Hebrew _Thou shalt go_, or, _It shall go_.
> - <sup>j</sup> 19. Or, _Who never more see_

## Chapter 50

### God the judge of the righteous and the wicked.

<sup>1</sup> > _A Psalm of Asaph._The Mighty One, God, Jehovah, hath spoken,<br>And called the earth from the rising of the sun unto the going down thereof.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Out of Zion, the perfection of beauty,<br>God hath shined forth.<br>
<sup>3</sup> Our God cometh, and doth not keep silence:<br>A fire devoureth before him,<br>And it is very tempestuous round about him.<br>
<sup>4</sup> He calleth to the heavens above,<br>And to the earth, that he may judge his people:<br>
<sup>5</sup> Gather my saints together unto me,<br>Those that have made a covenant with me by sacrifice.<br>
<sup>6</sup> And the heavens <sup>a</sup>shall declare his righteousness;<br>For God is judge himself. _[Selah_<br>
<sup>7</sup> Hear, O my people, and I will speak;<br>O Israel, and I will testify <sup>b</sup>unto thee:<br>I am God, _even_ thy God.<br>
<sup>8</sup> I will not reprove thee for thy sacrifices;<sup>c</sup><br>And thy burnt-offerings are continually before me.<br>
<sup>9</sup> I will take no bullock out of thy house,<br>Nor he-goats out of thy folds.<br>
<sup>10</sup> For every beast of the forest is mine,<br>And the cattle <sup>d</sup>upon a thousand hills.<br>
<sup>11</sup> I know all the birds of the mountains;<br>And the wild beasts of the field are <sup>e</sup>mine.<br>
<sup>12</sup> If I were hungry, I would not tell thee;<br>For the world is mine, and the fulness thereof.<br>
<sup>13</sup> Will I eat the flesh of bulls,<br>Or drink the blood of goats?<br>
<sup>14</sup> Offer unto God the sacrifice of thanksgiving;<br>And pay thy vows unto the Most High;<br>
<sup>15</sup> And call upon me in the day of trouble:<br>I will deliver thee, and thou shalt glorify me.<br>
<sup>16</sup> But unto the wicked God saith,<br>What hast thou to do to declare my statutes,<br>And that thou hast taken my covenant in thy mouth,<br>
<sup>17</sup> Seeing that thou hatest <sup>f</sup>instruction,<br>And castest my words behind thee?<br>
<sup>18</sup> When thou sawest a thief, thou consentedst with him,<br>And <sup>g</sup>hast been partaker with adulterers.<br>
<sup>19</sup> Thou givest thy mouth to evil,<br>And thy tongue frameth deceit.<br>
<sup>20</sup> Thou sittest and speakest against thy brother;<br>Thou <sup>h</sup>slanderest thine own mother’s son.<br>
<sup>21</sup> These things hast thou done, and I kept silence;<br>Thou thoughtest that I was altogether such a one as thyself:<br>_But_ I will reprove thee, and set _them_ in order before thine eyes.<br>
<sup>22</sup> Now consider this, ye that forget God,<br>Lest I tear you in pieces, and there be none to deliver:<br>
<sup>23</sup> Whoso offereth the sacrifice of thanksgiving glorifieth me;<br>And <sup>i</sup>to him that ordereth his way _aright_<br>Will I show the salvation of God.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 6. Or, _declare_
> - <sup>b</sup> 7. Or, _against_
> - <sup>c</sup> 8. Or, _Nor for thy burnt-offerings, which are etc._
> - <sup>d</sup> 10. Or, _upon the mountains where thousands are_
> - <sup>e</sup> 11. Or, _in my mind_. Hebrew _with me_.
> - <sup>f</sup> 17. Or, _correction_
> - <sup>g</sup> 18. Hebrew _thy portion was with adulterers_.
> - <sup>h</sup> 20. Or, _givest a thrust against_
> - <sup>i</sup> 23. Or, _prepareth a way that I may show him_

## Chapter 51

### A contrite sinner’s prayer for pardon.

<sup>1</sup> > _For the Chief Musician. A Psalm of David; when Nathan the prophet came unto him, after he had gone in to Bath-sheba._Have mercy upon me, O God, according to thy lovingkindness:<br>According to the multitude of thy tender mercies blot out my transgressions.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Wash me thoroughly from mine iniquity,<br>And cleanse me from my sin.<br>
<sup>3</sup> For I know my transgressions;<br>And my sin is ever before me.<br>
<sup>4</sup> Against thee, thee only, have I sinned,<br>And done that which is evil in thy sight;<br>That thou mayest be justified when thou speakest,<br>And be clear when thou judgest.<br>
<sup>5</sup> Behold, I was brought forth in iniquity;<br>And in sin did my mother conceive me.<br>
<sup>6</sup> Behold, thou desirest truth in the inward parts;<br>And in the hidden part thou wilt make me to know wisdom.<br>
<sup>7</sup> Purify me with hyssop, and I shall be clean:<br>Wash me, and I shall be whiter than snow.<br>
<sup>8</sup> Make me to hear joy and gladness,<br>That the bones which thou hast broken may rejoice.<br>
<sup>9</sup> Hide thy face from my sins,<br>And blot out all mine iniquities.<br>
<sup>10</sup> Create <sup>a</sup>in me a clean heart, O God;<br>And renew a <sup>b</sup>right spirit within me.<br>
<sup>11</sup> Cast me not away from thy presence;<br>And take not thy holy Spirit from me.<br>
<sup>12</sup> Restore unto me the joy of thy salvation;<br>And uphold me with a willing spirit.<br>
<sup>13</sup> Then will I teach transgressors thy ways;<br>And sinners shall <sup>c</sup>be converted unto thee.<br>
<sup>14</sup> Deliver me from bloodguiltiness, O God, thou God of my salvation;<br>_And_ my tongue shall sing aloud of thy righteousness.<br>
<sup>15</sup> O Lord, open thou my lips;<br>And my mouth shall show forth thy praise.<br>
<sup>16</sup> For thou delightest not in sacrifice; <sup>d</sup>else would I give it:<br>Thou hast no pleasure in burnt-offering.<br>
<sup>17</sup> The sacrifices of God are a broken spirit:<br>A broken and a contrite heart, O God, thou wilt not despise.<br>
<sup>18</sup> Do good in thy good pleasure unto Zion:<br>Build thou the walls of Jerusalem.<br>
<sup>19</sup> Then wilt thou delight in the sacrifices of righteousness,<br>In burnt-offering and whole burnt-offering:<br>Then will they offer bullocks upon thine altar.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 10. Or, _for me_
> - <sup>b</sup> 10. Or, _stedfast_
> - <sup>c</sup> 13. Or, _return_
> - <sup>d</sup> 16. Or, _that I should give it_

## Chapter 52

### Futility of boastful wickedness.

<sup>1</sup> > _For the Chief Musician. Maschil of David; when Doeg the Edomite came and told Saul, and said unto him, David is come to the house of Ahimelech._Why boastest thou thyself in mischief, O mighty man?<br>The lovingkindness of God _endureth_ continually.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Thy tongue deviseth very wickedness,<br>Like a sharp razor, working deceitfully.<br>
<sup>3</sup> Thou lovest evil more than good,<br>And lying rather than to speak righteousness. _[Selah_<br>
<sup>4</sup> Thou lovest all devouring words,<sup>a</sup><br>O thou deceitful tongue.<br>
<sup>5</sup> God will likewise <sup>b</sup>destroy thee for ever;<br>He will take thee up, and pluck thee out of thy tent,<br>And root thee out of the land of the living. _[Selah_<br>
<sup>6</sup> The righteous also shall see _it_, and fear,<br>And shall laugh at him, _saying_,<br>
<sup>7</sup> Lo, this is the man that made not God his <sup>c</sup>strength,<br>But trusted in the abundance of his riches,<br>And strengthened himself in his wickedness.<br>
<sup>8</sup> But as for me, I am like a green olive-tree in the house of God:<br>I trust in the lovingkindness of God for ever and ever.<br>
<sup>9</sup> I will give thee thanks for ever, because thou hast done it;<br>And I will hope in thy name, for it is good, in the presence of thy saints.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 4. Or, And _the deceitful tongue_
> - <sup>b</sup> 5. Or, _break thee down_
> - <sup>c</sup> 7. Or, _stronghold_

## Chapter 53

### The folly and wickedness of men.

<sup>1</sup> > _For the Chief Musician; set to Mahalath. Maschil of David._The fool hath said in his heart, There is no God.<br>Corrupt are they, and have done abominable iniquity;<br>There is none that doeth good.<br>
<sup>2</sup> God looked down from heaven upon the children of men,<br>To see if there were any that did <sup>a</sup>understand,<br>That did seek after God.<br>
<sup>3</sup> Every one of them is gone back; they are together become filthy;<br>There is none that doeth good, no, not one.<br>
<sup>4</sup> Have the workers of iniquity no knowledge,<br>Who eat up my people _as_ they eat bread,<br>And call not upon God?<br>
<sup>5</sup> There were they in great fear, where no fear was;<br>For God hath scattered the bones of him that encampeth against thee:<br>Thou hast put them to shame, because God hath rejected them.<br>
<sup>6</sup> Oh that the salvation of Israel were come out of Zion!<br>When God <sup>b</sup>bringeth back the captivity of his people,<br>Then shall Jacob rejoice, _and_ Israel shall be glad.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 2. Or, _deal wisely_
> - <sup>b</sup> 6. Or, _returneth to_

## Chapter 54

### Prayer for defence against enemies.

<sup>1</sup> > _For the Chief Musician; on stringed instruments. Maschil of David; when the Ziphites came and said to Saul, Doth not David hide himself with us?_Save me, O God, by thy name,<br>And judge me in thy might.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Hear my prayer, O God;<br>Give ear to the words of my mouth.<br>
<sup>3</sup> For strangers are risen up against me,<br>And violent men have sought after my soul:<br>They have not set God before them. _[Selah_<br>
<sup>4</sup> Behold, God is my helper:<br>The Lord is <sup>a</sup>of them that uphold my soul.<br>
<sup>5</sup> <sup>b</sup>He will requite the evil unto <sup>c</sup>mine enemies:<br>Destroy thou them in thy truth.<br>
<sup>6</sup> With a freewill-offering will I sacrifice unto thee:<br>I will give thanks unto thy name, O Jehovah, for it is good.<br>
<sup>7</sup> For he hath delivered me out of all trouble;<br>And mine eye hath seen _my desire_ upon mine enemies.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 4. Or, _with_
> - <sup>b</sup> 5. Another reading is, _The evil shall return_.
> - <sup>c</sup> 5. Or, _them that lie in wait for me_

## Chapter 55

### Prayer for the destruction of the treacherous.

<sup>1</sup> > _For the Chief Musician; on stringed instruments. Maschil of David._Give ear to my prayer, O God;<br>And hide not thyself from my supplication.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Attend unto me, and answer me:<br>I am restless in my complaint, and moan,<br>
<sup>3</sup> Because of the voice of the enemy,<br>Because of the oppression of the wicked;<br>For they cast iniquity upon me,<br>And in anger they persecute me.<br>
<sup>4</sup> My heart is sore pained within me:<br>And the terrors of death are fallen upon me.<br>
<sup>5</sup> Fearfulness and trembling are come upon me,<br>And horror hath overwhelmed me.<br>
<sup>6</sup> And I said, Oh that I had wings like a dove!<br>Then would I fly away, and be at rest.<br>
<sup>7</sup> Lo, then would I wander far off,<br>I would lodge in the wilderness. _[Selah_<br>
<sup>8</sup> I would <sup>a</sup>haste me to a shelter<br>From the stormy wind and tempest.<br>
<sup>9</sup> <sup>b</sup>Destroy, O Lord, _and_ divide their tongue;<br>For I have seen violence and strife in the city.<br>
<sup>10</sup> Day and night they go about it upon the walls thereof:<br>Iniquity also and mischief are in the midst of it.<br>
<sup>11</sup> Wickedness is in the midst thereof:<sup>c</sup><br>Oppression and guile depart not from its streets.<br>
<sup>12</sup> For it was not an enemy that reproached me;<br>Then I could have borne it:<br>Neither was it he that hated me that did magnify himself against me;<br>Then I would have hid myself from him:<br>
<sup>13</sup> But it was thou, a man mine equal,<br>My companion, and my familiar friend.<br>
<sup>14</sup> We took sweet counsel together;<br>We walked in the house of God with the throng.<br>
<sup>15</sup> <sup>d</sup>Let death come suddenly upon them,<br>Let them go down alive into Sheol;<br>For wickedness is in their dwelling, in <sup>e</sup>the midst of them.<br>
<sup>16</sup> As for me, I will call upon God;<br>And Jehovah will save me.<br>
<sup>17</sup> Evening, and morning, and at noonday, will I complain, and moan;<br>And he will hear my voice.<br>
<sup>18</sup> He hath redeemed my soul in peace <sup>f</sup>from the battle that was against me;<br>For they were many _that strove_ with me.<br>
<sup>19</sup> God will hear, and <sup>g</sup>answer them,<br>Even he that abideth of old, _[Selah_<br>_The men_ who have no changes,<br>And who fear not God.<br>
<sup>20</sup> He hath put forth his hands against such as were at peace with him:<br>He hath profaned his covenant.<br>
<sup>21</sup> His mouth was smooth as butter,<br>But his heart was war:<br>His words were softer than oil,<br>Yet were they drawn swords.<br>
<sup>22</sup> Cast <sup>h</sup>thy burden upon Jehovah, and he will sustain thee:<br>He will never suffer the righteous to be moved.<br>
<sup>23</sup> But thou, O God, wilt bring them down into the pit of destruction:<br>Bloodthirsty and deceitful men shall not live out half their days;<br>But I will trust in thee.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 8. Or, _hasten my escape_
> - <sup>b</sup> 9. Hebrew _Swallow up_.
> - <sup>c</sup> 11. Or, _Fraud_
> - <sup>d</sup> 15. Or, as otherwise read, _Desolations be upon them!_
> - <sup>e</sup> 15. Or, _their inward part_
> - <sup>f</sup> 18. Or, _so that none came nigh me_
> - <sup>g</sup> 19. Or, _afflict_
> - <sup>h</sup> 22. Or, _what he hath given thee_

## Chapter 56

### Supplication for deliverance, and grateful trust in God.

<sup>1</sup> > _For the Chief Musician; set to Jonath elem rehokim. _A Psalm_ of David. Michtam; when the Philistines took him in Gath._Be merciful unto me, O God; for man would swallow me up:<br>All the day long he fighting oppres<sup>a</sup>seth me.<br>
<sup>2</sup> <sup>b</sup>Mine enemies would swallow me up all the day long;<br>For they are many that fight proudly against me.<br>
<sup>3</sup> What time I am afraid,<br>I will put my trust in thee.<br>
<sup>4</sup> In God (I will praise his word),<br>In God have I put my trust, I will not be afraid;<br>What can flesh do unto me?<br>
<sup>5</sup> All the day long they wrest my words:<br>All their thoughts are against me for evil.<br>
<sup>6</sup> They gather themselves together, they hide themselves,<br>They mark my steps,<sup>c</sup><br>Even as they have waited for my soul.<br>
<sup>7</sup> <sup>d</sup>Shall they escape by iniquity?<br>In anger cast down the peoples, O God.<br>
<sup>8</sup> Thou numberest my wanderings:<br>Put thou my tears into thy bottle;<br>Are they not in thy <sup>e</sup>book?<br>
<sup>9</sup> Then shall mine enemies turn back in the day that I call:<br>This I know, <sup>f</sup>that God is for me.<br>
<sup>10</sup> In God (I will praise _his_ word),<br>In Jehovah (I will praise _his_ word),<br>
<sup>11</sup> In God have I put my trust, I will not be afraid;<br>What can man do unto me?<br>
<sup>12</sup> Thy vows are upon me, O God:<br>I will render thank-offerings unto thee.<br>
<sup>13</sup> For thou hast delivered my soul from death:<br>_Hast thou_ not _delivered_ my feet from falling,<br>That I may walk before God<br>In the light of <sup>g</sup>the living?<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 1. That is, _The silent dove of them that are afar off_. Or, as otherwise read, _The dove of the distant terebinths_.
> - <sup>b</sup> 2. Or, _They that lie in wait for me_
> - <sup>c</sup> 6. Or, _Inasmuch as_
> - <sup>d</sup> 7. Or, _They think to escape_
> - <sup>e</sup> 8. Or, _record_
> - <sup>f</sup> 9. Or, _for_
> - <sup>g</sup> 13. Or, _life_

## Chapter 57

### Prayer for rescue from persecutors.

<sup>1</sup> > _For the Chief Musician; _set to_ Al-tashheth. _A Psalm_ of David. Michtam; when he fled from Saul, in the cave._Be merciful unto me, O God, be merciful unto me;<br>For my soul taketh refuge in thee:<br>Yea, in the shadow of thy wings will I take refuge,<br>Until _these_ calamities be overpast.<sup>a</sup><br>
<sup>2</sup> I will cry unto God Most High,<br>Unto God that performeth _all things_ for me.<br>
<sup>3</sup> He will send from heaven, and save me,<br>_When_ he that would swallow me up reproacheth; _[Selah_<br>God will send forth his lovingkindness and his truth.<br>
<sup>4</sup> My soul is among lions;<br>I <sup>b</sup>lie among them that are set on fire,<br>Even the sons of men, whose teeth are spears and arrows,<br>And their tongue a sharp sword.<br>
<sup>5</sup> Be thou exalted, O God, above the heavens;<br>_Let_ thy glory _be_ above all the earth.<br>
<sup>6</sup> They have prepared a net for my steps;<br>My soul is bowed down:<br>They have digged a pit before me;<br>They are fallen into the midst thereof themselves. _[Selah_<br>
<sup>7</sup> My heart is fixed, O God, my heart is fixed:<br>I will sing, yea, I will sing praises.<br>
<sup>8</sup> Awake up, my glory; awake, psaltery and harp:<br>I myself <sup>c</sup>will awake right early.<br>
<sup>9</sup> I will give thanks unto thee, O Lord, among the peoples:<br>I will sing praises unto thee among the nations.<br>
<sup>10</sup> For thy lovingkindness is great unto the heavens,<br>And thy truth unto the skies.<br>
<sup>11</sup> Be thou exalted, O God, above the heavens;<br>_Let_ thy glory _be_ above all the earth.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 1. Or, _wickednesses_
> - <sup>b</sup> 4. Or, _must lie_
> - <sup>c</sup> 8. Or, _will awake the dawn_

## Chapter 58

### Prayer for the punishment of the wicked.

<sup>1</sup> > _For the Chief Musician; _set to_ Al-tashheth. _A Psalm_ of David. Michtam._Do ye indeed in silence speak righteousness?<br>Do ye judge uprightly, O ye sons of men?<sup>a</sup><sup>b</sup><sup>c</sup><br>
<sup>2</sup> Nay, in heart ye work wickedness;<br>Ye weigh out the violence of your hands in the earth.<br>
<sup>3</sup> The wicked are estranged from the womb:<br>They go astray as soon as they are born, speaking lies.<br>
<sup>4</sup> Their poison is like the poison of a serpent:<br>_They are_ like the deaf adder that stoppeth her ear,<br>
<sup>5</sup> Which hearkeneth not to the voice of <sup>d</sup>charmers,<br>Charming never so wisely.<br>
<sup>6</sup> Break their teeth, O God, in their mouth:<br>Break out the great teeth of the young lions, O Jehovah.<br>
<sup>7</sup> Let them melt away as water that runneth apace:<br>When he aimeth his arrows, let them be as though they were cut off.<br>
<sup>8</sup> _Let them be_ as a snail which melteth and passeth away,<br>_Like_ the untimely birth of a woman, <sup>e</sup>that hath not seen the sun.<br>
<sup>9</sup> Before your pots can feel the thorns,<sup>f</sup><br>He will take them away with a whirlwind, the green and the burning alike.<br>
<sup>10</sup> The righteous shall rejoice when he seeth the vengeance:<br>He shall wash his feet in the blood of the wicked;<br>
<sup>11</sup> So that men shall say, Verily there is <sup>g</sup>a reward for the righteous:<br>Verily there is a God that judgeth in the earth.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 1. Or, _Is the righteousness ye should speak dumb?_
> - <sup>b</sup> 1. Or, as otherwise read, _O ye gods_. Or, _O ye mighty ones_
> - <sup>c</sup> 1. Or, _judge uprightly the sons of men_
> - <sup>d</sup> 5. Or, _enchanters_
> - <sup>e</sup> 8. Or, like them _that have not seen the sun_
> - <sup>f</sup> 9. Or, _Wrath shall take them away while living as with a whirlwind_
> - <sup>g</sup> 11. Hebrew _fruit_.

## Chapter 59

### Prayer for deliverance from enemies.

<sup>1</sup> > _For the Chief Musician; _set to_ Al-tashheth. _A Psalm_ of David. Michtam; when Saul sent, and they watched the house to kill him._Deliver me from mine enemies, O my God:<br>Set me on high from them that rise up against me.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Deliver me from the workers of iniquity,<br>And save me from the bloodthirsty men.<br>
<sup>3</sup> For, lo, they lie in wait for my soul;<br>The mighty gather themselves together against me:<br>Not for my transgression, nor for my sin, O Jehovah.<br>
<sup>4</sup> They run and prepare themselves without _my_ fault:<br>Awake thou to <sup>a</sup>help me, and behold.<br>
<sup>5</sup> Even thou, O Jehovah God of hosts, the God of Israel,<br>Arise to visit all the nations:<br>Be not merciful to any wicked transgressors. _[Selah_<br>
<sup>6</sup> They return at evening, they howl like a dog,<br>And go round about the city.<br>
<sup>7</sup> Behold, they belch out with their mouth;<br>Swords are in their lips:<br>For who, _say they_, doth hear?<br>
<sup>8</sup> But thou, O Jehovah, wilt laugh at them;<br>Thou wilt have all the nations in derision.<br>
<sup>9</sup> <sup>b</sup>_Because of_ his strength I will give heed unto thee;<br>For God is my high tower.<br>
<sup>10</sup> My God with his lovingkindness will meet me:<br>God will let me see _my desire_ upon <sup>c</sup>mine enemies.<br>
<sup>11</sup> Slay them not, lest my people forget:<sup>d</sup><br>Scatter them by thy power, and bring them down,<br>O Lord our shield.<br>
<sup>12</sup> _For_ the sin of their mouth, _and_ the words of their lips,<br>Let them even be taken in their pride,<br>And for cursing and lying which they speak.<br>
<sup>13</sup> Consume them in wrath, consume them, so that they shall be no more:<br>And let them know that God ruleth in Jacob,<br>Unto the ends of the earth. _[Selah_<br>
<sup>14</sup> And at evening let them return, let them howl like a dog,<br>And go round about the city.<br>
<sup>15</sup> They shall wander up and down for food,<br>And tarry all night if they be not satisfied.<br>
<sup>16</sup> But I will sing of thy strength;<br>Yea, I will sing aloud of thy lovingkindness in the morning:<br>For thou hast been my high tower,<br>And a refuge in the day of my distress.<br>
<sup>17</sup> Unto thee, O my strength, will I sing praises:<br>For God is my high tower, the God of my mercy.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 4. Hebrew _meet_.
> - <sup>b</sup> 9. According to Septuagint and Vulgate, _My strength_.
> - <sup>c</sup> 10. Or, _them that lie in wait for me_
> - <sup>d</sup> 11. Or, _Make them wander to and fro_

## Chapter 60

### Lament over defeat in battle, and prayer for help.

<sup>1</sup> > _For the Chief Musician; set to Shushan Eduth. Michtam of David, to teach; when he strove with Aram-naharaim and with Aram-zobah, and Joab returned, and smote of Edom in the Valley of Salt twelve thousand._O God thou hast cast us off, thou hast broken us down;<br>Thou hast been angry; oh rest<sup>a</sup>ore us again.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Thou hast made the land to tremble; thou hast rent it:<br>Heal the breaches thereof; for it shaketh.<br>
<sup>3</sup> Thou hast showed thy people hard things:<br>Thou hast made us to drink the wine of staggering.<br>
<sup>4</sup> Thou hast given a banner to them that fear thee,<br>That it may be displayed because of the truth. _[Selah_<br>
<sup>5</sup> That thy beloved may be delivered,<br>Save with thy right hand, and answer <sup>b</sup>us.<br>
<sup>6</sup> God hath spoken in his holiness: I will exult;<br>I will divide Shechem, and mete out the valley of Succoth.<br>
<sup>7</sup> Gilead is mine, and Manasseh is mine;<br>Ephraim also is the defence of my head;<br>Judah is my <sup>c</sup>sceptre.<br>
<sup>8</sup> Moab is my washpot;<sup>d</sup><br>Upon Edom will I cast my shoe:<br>Philistia, shout thou because of me.<br>
<sup>9</sup> Who will bring me into the strong city?<sup>e</sup><br>Who hath led me unto Edom?<br>
<sup>10</sup> <sup>f</sup>Hast not thou, O God, cast us off?<br>And thou goest not forth, O God, with our hosts.<br>
<sup>11</sup> Give us help against the adversary;<br>For vain is the <sup>g</sup>help of man.<br>
<sup>12</sup> Through God we shall do valiantly;<br>For he it is that will tread down our adversaries.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 1. That is, _The lily of testimony_.
> - <sup>b</sup> 5. Another reading is, _me_.
> - <sup>c</sup> 7. Or, _lawgiver_
> - <sup>d</sup> 8. Or, _Unto_
> - <sup>e</sup> 9. Or, _Who will lead me etc._
> - <sup>f</sup> 10. Or, _Wilt not thou, O God, who hast cast us off, And goest . . . hosts?_
> - <sup>g</sup> 11. Hebrew _salvation_.

## Chapter 61

### Confidence in God’s protection.

<sup>1</sup> > _For the Chief Musician; on a stringed instrument. _A Psalm_ of David._Hear my cry, O God;<br>Attend unto my prayer.<br>
<sup>2</sup> From the end of the earth will I call unto thee, when my heart <sup>a</sup>is overwhelmed:<br>Lead me to <sup>b</sup>the rock that is higher than I.<br>
<sup>3</sup> For thou hast been a refuge for me,<br>A strong tower from the enemy.<br>
<sup>4</sup> I will dwell in thy <sup>c</sup>tabernacle for ever:<br>I will take refuge in the covert of thy wings. _[Selah_<br>
<sup>5</sup> For thou, O God, hast heard my vows:<br>Thou hast <sup>d</sup>given _me_ the heritage of those that fear thy name.<br>
<sup>6</sup> Thou wilt prolong the king’s life;<br>His years shall be as many generations.<br>
<sup>7</sup> He shall abide before God for ever:<br>Oh prepare lovingkindness and truth, that they may preserve him.<br>
<sup>8</sup> So will I sing praise unto thy name for ever,<br>That I may daily perform my vows.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 2. Or, _fainteth_
> - <sup>b</sup> 2. Or, _a rock that is too high for me_
> - <sup>c</sup> 4. Hebrew _tent_.
> - <sup>d</sup> 5. Or, _given a heritage unto those etc._

## Chapter 62

### God alone a refuge from treachery and oppression.

<sup>1</sup> > _For the Chief Musician; after the manner of Jeduthun. A Psalm of David._My soul waiteth in silence for God only:<br>From him _cometh_ my salvation.<sup>a</sup><br>
<sup>2</sup> He only is my rock and my salvation:<br>_He is_ my high tower; I shall not be greatly moved.<br>
<sup>3</sup> How long will ye set upon a man,<sup>b</sup><br>That ye may slay _him_, all of you,<br>Like a leaning wall, like a tottering fence?<br>
<sup>4</sup> They only consult to thrust him down from his dignity;<br>They delight in lies;<br>They bless with their mouth, but they curse inwardly. _[Selah_<br>
<sup>5</sup> My soul, <sup>c</sup>wait thou in silence for God only;<br>For my expectation is from him.<br>
<sup>6</sup> He only is my rock and my salvation:<br>_He is_ my high tower; I shall not be moved.<br>
<sup>7</sup> With God is my salvation and my glory:<br>The rock of my strength, and my refuge, is in God.<br>
<sup>8</sup> Trust in him at all times, ye people;<br>Pour out your heart before him:<br>God is a refuge for us. _[Selah_<br>
<sup>9</sup> Surely men of low degree are <sup>d</sup>vanity, and men of high degree are a lie:<br>In the balances they will go up;<br>They are together lighter than vanity.<br>
<sup>10</sup> Trust not in oppression,<br>And become not vain in robbery:<br>If riches increase, set not your heart _thereon_.<br>
<sup>11</sup> God hath spoken once,<br>Twice have I heard this,<br>That power belongeth unto God.<br>
<sup>12</sup> Also unto thee, O Lord, belongeth lovingkindness;<br>For thou renderest to every man according to his work.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 1. Hebrew _is silent unto God_.
> - <sup>b</sup> 3. Or, as otherwise read, _Ye shall be slain etc._
> - <sup>c</sup> 5. Hebrew _be thou silent unto God_.
> - <sup>d</sup> 9. Hebrew _a breath_.

## Chapter 63

### The thirsting soul satisfied in God.

<sup>1</sup> > _A Psalm of David, when he was in the wilderness of Judah._O God, thou art my God; earnestly will I seek thee:<br>My soul thirsteth for thee, my flesh longeth for thee,<br>In a dry and weary land, where no water is.<br>
<sup>2</sup> So have I looked upon thee in the sanctuary,<br>To see thy power and thy glory.<br>
<sup>3</sup> Because thy lovingkindness is better than life,<br>My lips shall praise thee.<br>
<sup>4</sup> So will I bless thee while I live:<br>I will lift up my hands in thy name.<br>
<sup>5</sup> My soul shall be satisfied as with <sup>a</sup>marrow and fatness;<br>And my mouth shall praise thee with joyful lips;<br>
<sup>6</sup> When I remember thee upon my bed,<sup>b</sup><br>_And_ meditate on thee in the night-watches.<br>
<sup>7</sup> For thou hast been my help,<br>And in the shadow of thy wings will I rejoice.<br>
<sup>8</sup> My soul followeth hard after thee:<br>Thy right hand upholdeth me.<br>
<sup>9</sup> But those that seek my soul, <sup>c</sup>to destroy it,<br>Shall go into the lower parts of the earth.<br>
<sup>10</sup> They shall be <sup>d</sup>given over to the power of the sword:<br>They shall be a portion for <sup>e</sup>foxes.<br>
<sup>11</sup> But the king shall rejoice in God:<br>Every one that sweareth by him shall glory;<br>For the mouth of them that speak lies shall be stopped.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 5. Hebrew _fat_.
> - <sup>b</sup> 6. Or, _I meditate_
> - <sup>c</sup> 9. Or, _shall be destroyed; They shall etc._
> - <sup>d</sup> 10. Or, _poured out by_
> - <sup>e</sup> 10. Or, _jackals_

## Chapter 64

### Prayer for deliverance from secret enemies.

<sup>1</sup> > _For the Chief Musician. A Psalm of David._Hear my voice, O God, in my complaint:<br>Preserve my life from fear of the enemy.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Hide me from the secret counsel of evil-doers,<br>From the <sup>a</sup>tumult of the workers of iniquity;<br>
<sup>3</sup> Who have whet their tongue like a sword,<br>And have aimed their arrows, even bitter words,<br>
<sup>4</sup> That they may shoot in secret places at the perfect:<br>Suddenly do they shoot at him, and fear not.<br>
<sup>5</sup> They encourage themselves in an evil purpose;<br>They commune of laying snares privily;<br>They say, Who will see them?<br>
<sup>6</sup> They search out iniquities;<sup>b</sup><br>We have accomplished, _say they_, a diligent search:<br>And the inward thought and the heart of every one is deep.<br>
<sup>7</sup> But God will shoot at them;<br>With an arrow suddenly shall they be wounded.<br>
<sup>8</sup> So <sup>c</sup>they shall be made to stumble, their own tongue being against them:<br>All that see them shall <sup>d</sup>wag the head.<br>
<sup>9</sup> And all men shall fear;<br>And they shall declare the work of God,<br>And shall wisely consider of his doing.<br>
<sup>10</sup> The righteous shall be glad in Jehovah, and shall take refuge in him;<br>And all the upright in heart shall glory.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 2. Or, _throng_
> - <sup>b</sup> 6. Or, as otherwise read, _They have accomplished_ (or _have hidden_)
> - <sup>c</sup> 8. Or, _shall they against whom their tongue was make them to stumble_
> - <sup>d</sup> 8. Or, _flee away_

## Chapter 65

### God’s abundant favor to earth and man.

<sup>1</sup> > _For the Chief Musician. A Psalm. A Song of David._Praise waiteth for thee, O God, in Zion;<br>And unto thee shall the vow be performed.<sup>a</sup><br>
<sup>2</sup> O thou that hearest prayer,<br>Unto thee shall all flesh come.<br>
<sup>3</sup> <sup>b</sup>Iniquities prevail against me:<br>As for our transgressions, thou wilt <sup>c</sup>forgive them.<br>
<sup>4</sup> Blessed is the man whom thou choosest, and causest to approach _unto thee_,<br>That he may dwell in thy courts:<br>We shall be satisfied with the goodness of thy house,<br>Thy holy temple.<br>
<sup>5</sup> By terrible things thou wilt answer us in righteousness,<br>O God of our salvation,<br>Thou that art the confidence of all the ends of the earth,<br>And <sup>d</sup>of them that are afar off upon the sea:<br>
<sup>6</sup> Who by his strength setteth fast the mountains,<br>Being girded about with might;<br>
<sup>7</sup> Who stilleth the roaring of the seas,<br>The roaring of their waves,<br>And the tumult of the peoples.<br>
<sup>8</sup> They also that dwell in the uttermost parts are afraid at thy tokens:<br>Thou makest the outgoings of the morning and evening to rejoice.<br>
<sup>9</sup> Thou visitest the earth, and waterest it,<br>Thou greatly enrichest it;<br>The river of God is full of water:<br>Thou providest them grain, <sup>e</sup>when thou hast so prepared <sup>f</sup>the earth.<br>
<sup>10</sup> Thou waterest its furrows abundantly;<br>Thou <sup>g</sup>settlest the ridges thereof:<br>Thou makest it soft with showers;<br>Thou blessest the springing thereof.<br>
<sup>11</sup> Thou crownest <sup>h</sup>the year with thy goodness;<br>And thy paths drop fatness.<br>
<sup>12</sup> <sup>i</sup>They drop upon the pastures of the wilderness;<br>And the hills are girded with joy.<br>
<sup>13</sup> The pastures are clothed with flocks;<br>The valleys also are covered over with grain;<br>They shout for joy, they also sing.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 1. Or, _There shall be silence before thee_, and _praise, O God etc._
> - <sup>b</sup> 3. Hebrew _Words_ (or _Matters_) _of iniquities_.
> - <sup>c</sup> 3. Or, _expiate_
> - <sup>d</sup> 5. Or, _of the sea, afar off_
> - <sup>e</sup> 9. Or, _for so preparest thou_
> - <sup>f</sup> 9. Hebrew _it_.
> - <sup>g</sup> 10. Hebrew _lowerest_.
> - <sup>h</sup> 11. Hebrew _the year of thy goodness_.
> - <sup>i</sup> 12. Or, _The pastures . . . do drop_

## Chapter 66

### Praise for God’s mighty deeds and for his answer to prayer.

<sup>1</sup> > _For the Chief Musician. A Song, a Psalm._Make a joyful noise unto God, all the earth:<br>
<sup>2</sup> Sing forth the glory of his name:<br>Make his praise glorious.<br>
<sup>3</sup> Say unto God, How terrible are thy works!<br>Through the greatness of thy power shall thine enemies <sup>a</sup>submit themselves unto thee.<br>
<sup>4</sup> All the earth shall worship thee,<br>And shall sing unto thee;<br>They shall sing to thy name. _[Selah_<br>
<sup>5</sup> Come, and see the works of God;<br>_He is_ terrible in his doing toward the children of men.<br>
<sup>6</sup> He turned the sea into dry land;<br>They went through the river on foot:<br>There <sup>b</sup>did we rejoice in him.<br>
<sup>7</sup> He ruleth by his might for ever;<br>His eyes observe the nations:<br>Let not the rebellious exalt themselves. _[Selah_<br>
<sup>8</sup> Oh bless our God, ye peoples,<br>And make the voice of his praise to be heard;<br>
<sup>9</sup> Who <sup>c</sup>holdeth our soul in life,<br>And suffereth not our feet to be moved.<br>
<sup>10</sup> For thou, O God, hast proved us:<br>Thou hast tried us, as silver is tried.<br>
<sup>11</sup> Thou broughtest us into the net;<br>Thou layedst a sore burden upon our loins.<br>
<sup>12</sup> Thou didst cause men to ride over our heads;<br>We went through fire and through water;<br>But thou broughtest us out into <sup>d</sup>a wealthy place.<br>
<sup>13</sup> I will come into thy house with burnt-offerings;<br>I will pay thee my vows,<br>
<sup>14</sup> Which my lips uttered,<br>And my mouth spake, when I was in distress.<br>
<sup>15</sup> I will offer unto thee burnt-offerings of fatlings,<br>With the incense of rams;<br>I will offer bullocks with goats. _[Selah_<br>
<sup>16</sup> Come, and hear, all ye that fear God,<br>And I will declare what he hath done for my soul.<br>
<sup>17</sup> I cried unto him with my mouth,<br>And <sup>e</sup>he was extolled with my tongue.<br>
<sup>18</sup> If I <sup>f</sup>regard iniquity in my heart,<br>The Lord <sup>g</sup>will not hear:<br>
<sup>19</sup> But verily God hath heard;<br>He hath attended to the voice of my prayer.<br>
<sup>20</sup> Blessed be God,<br>Who hath not turned away my prayer,<br>Nor his lovingkindness from me.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 3. Or, _yield feigned obedience_. Hebrew _lie_.
> - <sup>b</sup> 6. Or, _let us rejoice_
> - <sup>c</sup> 9. Hebrew _putteth_.
> - <sup>d</sup> 12. Hebrew _abundance_.
> - <sup>e</sup> 17. Or, as otherwise read, _high praise was under my tongue_
> - <sup>f</sup> 18. Or, _had regarded_
> - <sup>g</sup> 18. Or, _would_

## Chapter 67

### The nations exhorted to praise God.

<sup>1</sup> > _For the Chief Musician; on stringed instruments. A Psalm, a Song._God be merciful unto us, and bless us,<br>_And_ cause his face to shine upon us; _[Selah_<sup>a</sup><br>
<sup>2</sup> That thy way may be known upon earth,<br>Thy salvation among all nations.<br>
<sup>3</sup> Let the peoples <sup>b</sup>praise thee, O God;<br>Let all the peoples praise thee.<br>
<sup>4</sup> Oh let the nations be glad and sing for joy;<br>For thou wilt judge the peoples with equity,<br>And <sup>c</sup>govern the nations upon earth. _[Selah_<br>
<sup>5</sup> Let the peoples praise thee, O God;<br>Let all the peoples praise thee.<br>
<sup>6</sup> The earth hath yielded its increase:<br>God, even our own God, will bless us.<br>
<sup>7</sup> God will bless us;<br>And all the ends of the earth shall fear him.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 1. Hebrew _with us_.
> - <sup>b</sup> 3. Or, _give thanks unto_
> - <sup>c</sup> 4. Hebrew _lead_.

## Chapter 68

### Jehovah the God of Sinai and of the sanctuary.

<sup>1</sup> > _For the Chief Musician; A Psalm of David, a Song._Let God arise, let his enemies be scattered;<br>Let them also that hate him flee before him.<sup>a</sup><br>
<sup>2</sup> As smoke is driven away, so drive them away:<br>As wax melteth before the fire,<br>So let the wicked perish at the presence of God.<br>
<sup>3</sup> But let the righteous be glad; let them exult before God:<br>Yea, let them rejoice with gladness.<br>
<sup>4</sup> Sing unto God, sing praises to his name:<br>Cast up a highway for him that rideth through the deserts;<br>His name is <sup>b</sup>Jehovah; and exult ye before him.<br>
<sup>5</sup> A father of the fatherless, and a judge of the widows,<br>Is God in his holy habitation.<br>
<sup>6</sup> God <sup>c</sup>setteth the solitary in families:<br>He bringeth out the prisoners into prosperity;<br>But the rebellious dwell in a parched land.<br>
<sup>7</sup> O God, when thou wentest forth before thy people,<br>When thou didst march through the wilderness; _[Selah_<br>
<sup>8</sup> The earth trembled,<br>The heavens also dropped _rain_ at the presence of God:<br>Yon Sinai _trembled_ at the presence of God, the God of Israel.<br>
<sup>9</sup> Thou, O God, didst send a plentiful rain,<br>Thou didst confirm thine inheritance, when it was weary.<br>
<sup>10</sup> Thy <sup>d</sup>congregation dwelt therein:<br>Thou, O God, didst prepare of thy goodness for the poor.<br>
<sup>11</sup> The Lord giveth the word:<br>The women that publish the tidings are a great host.<br>
<sup>12</sup> Kings of armies flee, they flee;<br>And she that tarrieth at home divideth the spoil.<br>
<sup>13</sup> <sup>e</sup>When ye lie among the sheepfolds,<br>_It is as_ the wings of a dove covered with silver,<br>And her pinions with yellow gold.<br>
<sup>14</sup> When the Almighty scattered kings therein,<br>_It_ <sup>f</sup>_was as when_ it snoweth in Zalmon.<br>
<sup>15</sup> A mountain of God is the mountain of Bashan;<br>A <sup>g</sup>high mountain is the mountain of Bashan.<br>
<sup>16</sup> Why look ye askance, ye high mountains,<br>At the mountain which God hath desired for his abode?<br>Yea, Jehovah will dwell _in it_ for ever.<br>
<sup>17</sup> The chariots of God are twenty thousand, even thousands upon thousands:<br>The Lord is among them, <sup>h</sup>_as in_ Sinai, in the sanctuary.<br>
<sup>18</sup> Thou hast ascended on high, thou hast led away captives;<br>Thou hast received gifts among men,<br>Yea, _among_ the rebellious also, that <sup>i</sup>Jehovah God might <sup>j</sup>dwell _with them_.<br>
<sup>19</sup> Blessed be <sup>k</sup>the Lord, who daily beareth our burden,<br>Even the God who is our salvation. _[Selah_<br>
<sup>20</sup> God is unto us a God of deliverances;<br>And unto Jehovah the Lord belongeth escape from death.<br>
<sup>21</sup> But God will smite through the head of his enemies,<br>The hairy scalp of such a one as goeth on still in his guiltiness.<br>
<sup>22</sup> The Lord said, I will bring again from Bashan,<br>I will bring _them_ again from the depths of the sea;<br>
<sup>23</sup> That thou mayest crush _them_, _dipping_ thy foot in blood,<br>That the tongue of thy dogs may have its portion from _thine_ enemies.<br>
<sup>24</sup> They have seen thy goings, O God,<br>Even the goings of my God, my King, <sup>l</sup>into the sanctuary.<br>
<sup>25</sup> The singers went before, the minstrels followed after,<br>In the midst of the damsels playing with timbrels.<br>
<sup>26</sup> Bless ye God in the congregations,<br>Even the Lord, _ye that are_ of the fountain of Israel.<br>
<sup>27</sup> There is little Benjamin their ruler,<br>The princes of Judah _and_ their <sup>m</sup>council,<br>The princes of Zebulun, the princes of Naphtali.<br>
<sup>28</sup> Thy God hath commanded thy strength:<sup>n</sup><br>Strengthen, O God, that which thou <sup>o</sup>hast wrought for us.<br>
<sup>29</sup> Because of thy temple at Jerusalem<br>Kings shall bring presents unto thee.<br>
<sup>30</sup> Rebuke the wild beast of the reeds,<br>The multitude of the bulls, with the calves of the peoples,<sup>p</sup><br>Trampling under foot the pieces of silver:<sup>q</sup><br>He hath scattered the peoples that delight in war.<br>
<sup>31</sup> Princes shall come out of Egypt;<sup>r</sup><br>Ethiopia shall haste to stretch out her hands unto God.<br>
<sup>32</sup> Sing unto God, ye kingdoms of the earth;<br>Oh sing praises unto the Lord; _[Selah_<br>
<sup>33</sup> To him that rideth upon the heaven of heavens, which are of old;<br>Lo, he uttereth his voice, a mighty voice.<br>
<sup>34</sup> Ascribe ye strength unto God:<br>His excellency is over Israel,<br>And his strength is in the skies.<br>
<sup>35</sup> <sup>s</sup>O God, _thou art_ terrible out of thy holy places:<br>The God of Israel, he giveth strength and power unto _his_ people.<br>Blessed be God.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 1. Or, _God ariseth_; and so throughout verses 1, 2, 3
> - <sup>b</sup> 4. Hebrew _Jah_.
> - <sup>c</sup> 6. Hebrew _maketh the solitary to dwell in a house_.
> - <sup>d</sup> 10. Or, _troop_
> - <sup>e</sup> 13. Or, _Will ye lie among the sheepfolds_, as _the wings . . . gold?_
> - <sup>f</sup> 14. Or, _snowed_
> - <sup>g</sup> 15. Hebrew _mountain of summits_.
> - <sup>h</sup> 17. Or, _Sinai_ is _in the sanctuary_
> - <sup>i</sup> 18. Hebrew _Jah_.
> - <sup>j</sup> 18. Or, _dwell_ there
> - <sup>k</sup> 19. Or, _the Lord day by day: if one oppresseth us, God is our salvation_
> - <sup>l</sup> 24. Or, _in the sanctuary_. Or, _in holiness_
> - <sup>m</sup> 27. Or, _company_
> - <sup>n</sup> 28. Or, _Be strong, O God, thou that hast etc._
> - <sup>o</sup> 28. Or, _hast wrought for us out of thy temple. Unto Jerusalem etc._
> - <sup>p</sup> 30. Or, Every one _submitting himself with pieces of silver_
> - <sup>q</sup> 30. Or, as otherwise read, _Scatter thou_
> - <sup>r</sup> 31. Hebrew _Cush_.
> - <sup>s</sup> 35. Or, _Terrible is God_

## Chapter 69

### A cry of distress, and imprecation on adversaries.

<sup>1</sup> > _For the Chief Musician; set to Shoshannim. _A Psalm_ of David._Save me, O God;<br>For the waters are come in unto my soul.<sup>a</sup><br>
<sup>2</sup> I sink in deep mire, where there is no standing:<br>I am come into deep waters, where the floods overflow me.<br>
<sup>3</sup> I am weary with my crying; my throat is dried:<br>Mine eyes fail while I wait for my God.<br>
<sup>4</sup> They that hate me without a cause are more than the hairs of my head:<br>They that would cut me off, being mine enemies <sup>b</sup>wrongfully, are mighty:<br>That which I took not away I have to restore.<br>
<sup>5</sup> O God, thou knowest my foolishness;<br>And my <sup>c</sup>sins are not hid from thee.<br>
<sup>6</sup> Let not them that wait for thee be put to shame through me, O Lord Jehovah of hosts:<br>Let not those that seek thee be brought to dishonor through me, O God of Israel.<br>
<sup>7</sup> Because for thy sake I have borne reproach;<br>Shame hath covered my face.<br>
<sup>8</sup> I am become a stranger unto my brethren,<br>And an alien unto my mother’s children.<br>
<sup>9</sup> For the zeal of thy house hath eaten me up;<br>And the reproaches of them that reproach thee are fallen upon me.<br>
<sup>10</sup> When I wept, _and chastened_ my soul with fasting,<br>That was to my reproach.<br>
<sup>11</sup> When I made sackcloth my clothing,<br>I became a byword unto them.<br>
<sup>12</sup> They that sit in the gate talk of me;<br>And _I am_ the song of the drunkards.<br>
<sup>13</sup> But as for me, my prayer is unto thee, O Jehovah, in an acceptable time:<br>O God, in the abundance of thy lovingkindness,<br>Answer me in the truth of thy salvation.<br>
<sup>14</sup> Deliver me out of the mire, and let me not sink:<br>Let me be delivered from them that hate me, and out of the deep waters.<br>
<sup>15</sup> Let not the waterflood overwhelm me,<br>Neither let the deep swallow me up;<br>And let not the pit shut its mouth upon me.<br>
<sup>16</sup> Answer me, O Jehovah; for thy lovingkindness is good:<br>According to the multitude of thy tender mercies turn thou unto me.<br>
<sup>17</sup> And hide not thy face from thy servant;<br>For I am in distress; answer me speedily.<br>
<sup>18</sup> Draw nigh unto my soul, and redeem it:<br>Ransom me because of mine enemies.<br>
<sup>19</sup> Thou knowest my reproach, and my shame, and my dishonor:<br>Mine adversaries are all before thee.<br>
<sup>20</sup> Reproach hath broken my heart; and I am <sup>d</sup>full of heaviness:<br>And I looked for some to take pity, but there was none;<br>And for comforters, but I found none.<br>
<sup>21</sup> They gave me also gall for my food;<br>And in my thirst they gave me vinegar to drink.<br>
<sup>22</sup> Let their table before them become a snare;<br>And when they are in peace, _let it become_ a trap.<br>
<sup>23</sup> Let their eyes be darkened, so that they cannot see;<br>And make their loins continually to shake.<br>
<sup>24</sup> Pour out thine indignation upon them,<br>And let the fierceness of thine anger overtake them.<br>
<sup>25</sup> Let their <sup>e</sup>habitation be desolate;<br>Let none dwell in their tents.<br>
<sup>26</sup> For they persecute him whom thou hast smitten;<br>And they tell of the <sup>f</sup>sorrow of those whom thou hast wounded.<br>
<sup>27</sup> Add iniquity unto their iniquity;<br>And let them not come into thy righteousness.<br>
<sup>28</sup> Let them be blotted out of the book of <sup>g</sup>life,<br>And not be written with the righteous.<br>
<sup>29</sup> But I am poor and <sup>h</sup>sorrowful:<br>Let thy salvation, O God, set me up on high.<br>
<sup>30</sup> I will praise the name of God with a song,<br>And will magnify him with thanksgiving.<br>
<sup>31</sup> And it will please Jehovah better than an ox,<br>_Or_ a bullock that hath horns and hoofs.<br>
<sup>32</sup> The meek have seen it, and are glad:<br>Ye that seek after God, let your heart live.<br>
<sup>33</sup> For Jehovah heareth the needy,<br>And despiseth not his prisoners.<br>
<sup>34</sup> Let heaven and earth praise him,<br>The seas, and everything that moveth therein.<br>
<sup>35</sup> For God will save Zion, and build the cities of Judah;<br>And they shall abide there, and have it in possession.<br>
<sup>36</sup> The seed also of his servants shall inherit it;<br>And they that love his name shall dwell therein.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 1. That is, _Lilies_.
> - <sup>b</sup> 4. Hebrew _falsely_.
> - <sup>c</sup> 5. Hebrew _guiltinesses_.
> - <sup>d</sup> 20. Or, _sore sick_
> - <sup>e</sup> 25. Or, _encampment_
> - <sup>f</sup> 26. Or, _pain_
> - <sup>g</sup> 28. Or, _the living_
> - <sup>h</sup> 29. Or, _in pain_

## Chapter 70

### Prayer for help against persecutors.

<sup>1</sup> > _For the Chief Musician. _A Psalm_ of David; to bring to remembrance.__Make haste_, O God, to deliver me;<br>Make haste to help me, O Jehovah.<sup>a</sup><br>
<sup>2</sup> Let them be put to shame and confounded<br>That seek after my soul:<br>Let them be turned backward and brought to dishonor<br>That delight in my hurt.<br>
<sup>3</sup> Let them be turned back <sup>b</sup>by reason of their shame<br>That say, Aha, aha.<br>
<sup>4</sup> Let all those that seek thee rejoice and be glad in thee;<br>And let such as love thy salvation say continually,<br>Let God be magnified.<br>
<sup>5</sup> But I am poor and needy;<br>Make haste unto me, O God:<br>Thou art my help and my deliverer;<br>O Jehovah, make no tarrying.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 1. Or, _to make memorial_
> - <sup>b</sup> 3. Or, _for a reward of_

## Chapter 71

### Prayer of an old man for deliverance.

<sup>1</sup> In thee, O Jehovah, do I take refuge:<br>Let me never be put to shame.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Deliver me in thy righteousness, and rescue me:<br>Bow down thine ear unto me, and save me.<br>
<sup>3</sup> Be thou to me a rock of habitation, whereunto I may continually resort:<br>Thou hast given commandment to save me;<br>For thou art my rock and my fortress.<br>
<sup>4</sup> Rescue me, O my God, out of the hand of the wicked,<br>Out of the hand of the unrighteous and cruel man.<br>
<sup>5</sup> For thou art my hope, O Lord Jehovah:<br>_Thou art_ my trust from my youth.<br>
<sup>6</sup> By thee have I been holden up from the womb;<br>Thou <sup>a</sup>art he that took me out of my mother’s bowels:<br>My praise shall be continually of thee.<br>
<sup>7</sup> I am as a wonder unto many;<br>But thou art my strong refuge.<br>
<sup>8</sup> My mouth shall be filled with thy praise,<br>And with thy honor all the day.<br>
<sup>9</sup> Cast me not off in the time of old age;<br>Forsake me not when my strength faileth.<br>
<sup>10</sup> For mine enemies speak concerning me;<br>And they that watch for my soul take counsel together,<br>
<sup>11</sup> Saying, God hath forsaken him:<br>Pursue and take him; for there is none to deliver.<br>
<sup>12</sup> O God, be not far from me;<br>O my God, make haste to help me.<br>
<sup>13</sup> Let them be put to shame _and_ consumed that are adversaries to my soul;<br>Let them be covered with reproach and dishonor that seek my hurt.<br>
<sup>14</sup> But I will hope continually,<br>And will praise thee yet more and more.<br>
<sup>15</sup> My mouth shall tell of thy righteousness,<br>_And_ of thy salvation all the day;<br>For I know not the numbers _thereof_.<br>
<sup>16</sup> I will come <sup>b</sup>with the mighty acts of the Lord Jehovah:<br>I will make mention of thy righteousness, even of thine only.<br>
<sup>17</sup> O God, thou hast taught me from my youth;<br>And hitherto have I declared thy wondrous works.<br>
<sup>18</sup> Yea, even <sup>c</sup>when I am old and grayheaded, O God, forsake me not,<br>Until I have declared <sup>d</sup>thy strength unto _the next_ generation,<br>Thy might to every one that is to come.<br>
<sup>19</sup> Thy righteousness also, O God, is very high;<br>Thou who hast done great things, O God, who is like unto thee?<br>
<sup>20</sup> Thou, who hast showed <sup>e</sup>us many and sore troubles,<br>Wilt quicken <sup>f</sup>us again,<br>And wilt bring <sup>g</sup>us up again from the depths of the earth.<br>
<sup>21</sup> Increase thou my greatness,<br>And turn again and comfort me.<br>
<sup>22</sup> I will also praise thee with the psaltery,<br>_Even_ thy truth, O my God:<br>Unto thee will I sing praises with the harp,<br>O thou Holy One of Israel.<br>
<sup>23</sup> My lips shall shout for joy when I sing praises unto thee;<br>And my soul, which thou hast redeemed.<br>
<sup>24</sup> My tongue also shall talk of thy righteousness all the day long;<br>For they are put to shame, for they are confounded, that seek my hurt.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 6. Or, _hast been my benefactor from etc._
