> - <sup>b</sup> 16. Or, _in the strength_
> - <sup>c</sup> 18. Hebrew _unto old age and gray hairs_.
> - <sup>d</sup> 18. Hebrew _thine arm_.
> - <sup>e</sup> 20. Another reading is, _me_.
> - <sup>f</sup> 20. Another reading is, _me_.
> - <sup>g</sup> 20. Another reading is, _me_.

## Chapter 72

### The reign of the righteous king.

<sup>1</sup> > __A Psalm_ of Solomon._Give the king thy judgments, O God,<br>And thy righteousness unto the king’s son.<br>
<sup>2</sup> <sup>a</sup>He will judge thy people with righteousness,<br>And thy poor with justice.<br>
<sup>3</sup> The mountains shall bring peace to the people,<br>And the hills, in righteousness.<br>
<sup>4</sup> He will judge the poor of the people,<br>He will save the children of the needy,<br>And will break in pieces the oppressor.<br>
<sup>5</sup> They shall fear thee while the sun endureth,<br>And <sup>b</sup>so long as the moon, throughout all generations.<br>
<sup>6</sup> He will come down like rain upon the mown grass,<br>As showers that water the earth.<br>
<sup>7</sup> In his days shall the righteous flourish,<br>And abundance of peace, till the moon be no more.<br>
<sup>8</sup> He shall have dominion also from sea to sea,<br>And from the River unto the ends of the earth.<br>
<sup>9</sup> They that dwell in the wilderness shall bow before him;<br>And his enemies shall lick the dust.<br>
<sup>10</sup> The kings of Tarshish and of the isles shall render tribute:<br>The kings of Sheba and Seba shall offer gifts.<br>
<sup>11</sup> Yea, all kings shall fall down before him;<br>All nations shall serve him.<br>
<sup>12</sup> For he will deliver the needy when he crieth,<br>And the poor, <sup>c</sup>that hath no helper.<br>
<sup>13</sup> He will have pity on the <sup>d</sup>poor and needy,<br>And the souls of the needy he will save.<br>
<sup>14</sup> He will redeem their soul from <sup>e</sup>oppression and violence;<br>And precious will their blood be in his sight:<br>
<sup>15</sup> And <sup>f</sup>they shall live; and to him shall be given of the gold of Sheba:<br>And men shall pray for him continually;<br>They shall bless him all the day long.<br>
<sup>16</sup> There shall be <sup>g</sup>abundance of grain in the <sup>h</sup>earth upon the top of the mountains;<br>The fruit thereof shall shake like Lebanon:<br>And they of the city shall flourish like grass of the earth.<br>
<sup>17</sup> His name shall endure for ever;<br>His name shall <sup>i</sup>be continued <sup>j</sup>as long as the sun:<br>And men shall <sup>k</sup>be blessed in him;<br>All nations shall call him happy.<br>
<sup>18</sup> Blessed be Jehovah God, the God of Israel,<br>Who only doeth wondrous things:<br>
<sup>19</sup> And blessed be his glorious name for ever;<br>And let the whole earth be filled with his glory.<br>Amen, and Amen.<br>
<sup>20</sup> The prayers of David the son of Jesse are ended.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 2. Or, _Let him_ (and so throughout the Psalm)
> - <sup>b</sup> 5. Hebrew _before the moon_.
> - <sup>c</sup> 12. Or, _and him that hath_
> - <sup>d</sup> 13. Or, _weak_
> - <sup>e</sup> 14. Or, _fraud_
> - <sup>f</sup> 15. Or, _he_
> - <sup>g</sup> 16. Or, _a handful_
> - <sup>h</sup> 16. Or, _land_
> - <sup>i</sup> 17. Or, _have issue_
> - <sup>j</sup> 17. Hebrew _before the sun_.
> - <sup>k</sup> 17. Or, _bless themselves_

## Chapter 73

### The end of the wicked contrasted with that of the righteous.

<sup>1</sup> > _A Psalm of Asaph._Surely God is good to Israel,<br>_Even_ to such as are pure in heart.<sup>a</sup><br>
<sup>2</sup> But as for me, my feet were almost gone;<br>My steps had well nigh slipped.<br>
<sup>3</sup> For I was envious at the <sup>b</sup>arrogant,<br>When I saw the prosperity of the wicked.<br>
<sup>4</sup> For there are no pangs in their death;<br>But their strength is firm.<br>
<sup>5</sup> They are not <sup>c</sup>in trouble as _other_ men;<br>Neither are they plagued like _other_ men.<br>
<sup>6</sup> Therefore pride is as a chain about their neck;<br>Violence covereth them as a garment.<br>
<sup>7</sup> Their eyes stand out with fatness:<sup>d</sup><br>They have more than heart could wish.<br>
<sup>8</sup> They scoff, and in wickedness utter oppression:<br>They speak <sup>e</sup>loftily.<br>
<sup>9</sup> They have set their mouth <sup>f</sup>in the heavens,<br>And their tongue walketh through the earth.<br>
<sup>10</sup> Therefore <sup>g</sup>his people return hither:<br>And waters of a full _cup_ are drained by them.<br>
<sup>11</sup> And they say, How doth God know?<br>And is there knowledge in the Most High?<br>
<sup>12</sup> Behold, these are the wicked;<br>And, being alway at ease, they increase in riches.<br>
<sup>13</sup> Surely in vain have I cleansed my heart,<br>And washed my hands in innocency;<br>
<sup>14</sup> For all the day long have I been plagued,<br>And <sup>h</sup>chastened every morning.<br>
<sup>15</sup> If I had said, I will speak thus;<br>Behold, I had dealt treacherously with the generation of thy children.<br>
<sup>16</sup> When I thought how I might know this,<br>It was <sup>i</sup>too painful for me;<br>
<sup>17</sup> Until I went into the sanctuary of God,<br>And considered their latter end.<br>
<sup>18</sup> Surely thou settest them in slippery places:<br>Thou castest them down to <sup>j</sup>destruction.<br>
<sup>19</sup> How are they become a desolation in a moment!<br>They are utterly consumed with terrors.<br>
<sup>20</sup> As a dream when one awaketh,<br>So, O Lord, <sup>k</sup>when thou awakest, thou wilt despise their image.<br>
<sup>21</sup> For my soul <sup>l</sup>was grieved,<br>And I was pricked in my <sup>m</sup>heart:<br>
<sup>22</sup> So brutish <sup>n</sup>was I, and ignorant;<br>I was _as_ a beast <sup>o</sup>before thee.<br>
<sup>23</sup> Nevertheless I am continually with thee:<br>Thou hast holden my right hand.<br>
<sup>24</sup> Thou wilt guide me with thy counsel,<br>And afterward receive me <sup>p</sup>to glory.<br>
<sup>25</sup> Whom have I in heaven _but thee_?<br>And there is none upon earth that I desire <sup>q</sup>besides thee.<br>
<sup>26</sup> My flesh and my heart faileth;<br>_But_ God is the <sup>r</sup>strength of my heart and my portion for ever.<br>
<sup>27</sup> For, lo, they that are far from thee shall perish:<br>Thou hast destroyed all them that play the harlot, _departing_ from thee.<br>
<sup>28</sup> But it is good for me to draw near unto God:<br>I have made the Lord Jehovah my refuge,<br>That I may tell of all thy works.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 1. Or, _Only good is God_
> - <sup>b</sup> 3. Or, _fools_
> - <sup>c</sup> 5. Hebrew _in the trouble of men_.
> - <sup>d</sup> 7. Or, _The imaginations of their heart overflow_
> - <sup>e</sup> 8. Or, _from on high_
> - <sup>f</sup> 9. Or, _against_
> - <sup>g</sup> 10. Another reading is, _he will bring back his people_.
> - <sup>h</sup> 14. Hebrew _my chastisement_ was.
> - <sup>i</sup> 16. Hebrew _labor in mine eyes_.
> - <sup>j</sup> 18. Hebrew _ruins_.
> - <sup>k</sup> 20. Or, _in the city_
> - <sup>l</sup> 21. Hebrew _was in a ferment_.
> - <sup>m</sup> 21. Hebrew _reins_.
> - <sup>n</sup> 22. Or, _am_
> - <sup>o</sup> 22. Hebrew _with thee_.
> - <sup>p</sup> 24. Or, _with_
> - <sup>q</sup> 25. Or, _with thee_
> - <sup>r</sup> 26. Hebrew _rock_.

## Chapter 74

### A complaint over the devastation of the land by the enemy.

<sup>1</sup> > _Maschil of Asaph._O God, why hast thou cast _us_ off for ever?<br>Why doth thine anger smoke against the sheep of thy pasture?<br>
<sup>2</sup> Remember thy congregation, which thou hast gotten of old,<br>Which thou hast redeemed to be the tribe of thine inheritance;<br>_And_ mount Zion, wherein thou hast dwelt.<br>
<sup>3</sup> Lift up thy feet unto the perpetual ruins,<sup>a</sup><br>All the evil that the enemy hath done in the sanctuary.<br>
<sup>4</sup> Thine adversaries have roared in the midst of thine assembly;<br>They have set up their ensigns for signs.<br>
<sup>5</sup> They <sup>b</sup>seemed as men that lifted up<br>Axes upon a thicket of trees.<br>
<sup>6</sup> And now all the carved work thereof<br>They break down with hatchet and hammers.<br>
<sup>7</sup> They have set thy sanctuary on fire;<br>They have profaned the dwelling-place of thy name _by casting it_ to the ground.<br>
<sup>8</sup> They said in their heart, Let us make havoc of them altogether:<br>They have burned up all the <sup>c</sup>synagogues of God in the land.<br>
<sup>9</sup> We see not our signs:<br>There is no more any prophet;<br>Neither is there among us any that knoweth how long.<br>
<sup>10</sup> How long, O God, shall the adversary reproach?<br>Shall the enemy blaspheme thy name for ever?<br>
<sup>11</sup> Why drawest thou back thy hand, even thy right hand?<br>_Pluck it_ out of thy bosom _and_ consume _them_.<br>
<sup>12</sup> Yet God is my King of old,<br>Working salvation in the midst of the earth.<br>
<sup>13</sup> Thou didst <sup>d</sup>divide the sea by thy strength:<br>Thou brakest the heads of the sea-monsters in the waters.<br>
<sup>14</sup> Thou brakest the heads of leviathan in pieces;<br>Thou gavest him to be food to the people inhabiting the wilderness.<br>
<sup>15</sup> Thou didst cleave fountain and flood:<br>Thou driedst up <sup>e</sup>mighty rivers.<br>
<sup>16</sup> The day is thine, the night also is thine:<br>Thou hast prepared the <sup>f</sup>light and the sun.<br>
<sup>17</sup> Thou hast set all the borders of the earth:<br>Thou hast made summer and winter.<br>
<sup>18</sup> Remember this, that the enemy <sup>g</sup>hath reproached, O Jehovah,<br>And that a foolish people hath blasphemed thy name.<br>
<sup>19</sup> Oh deliver not <sup>h</sup>the soul of thy turtle-dove unto the wild beast:<br>Forget not the <sup>i</sup>life of thy poor for ever.<br>
<sup>20</sup> Have respect unto the covenant;<br>For the dark places of the <sup>j</sup>earth are full of the habitations of violence.<br>
<sup>21</sup> Oh let not the oppressed return ashamed:<br>Let the poor and needy praise thy name.<br>
<sup>22</sup> Arise, O God, plead thine own cause:<br>Remember how the foolish man reproacheth thee all the day.<br>
<sup>23</sup> Forget not the voice of thine adversaries:<br>The tumult of those that rise up against thee <sup>k</sup>ascendeth continually.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 3. Or, _The enemy hath wrought all evil_
> - <sup>b</sup> 5. Or, _made themselves known_
> - <sup>c</sup> 8. Or, _places of assembly_
> - <sup>d</sup> 13. Hebrew _break up_.
> - <sup>e</sup> 15. Or, _ever-flowing_
> - <sup>f</sup> 16. Hebrew _luminary_.
> - <sup>g</sup> 18. Or, _hath reproached Jehovah_
> - <sup>h</sup> 19. Or, _thy turtle-dove unto the greedy multitude_
> - <sup>i</sup> 19. Or, _multitude_
> - <sup>j</sup> 20. Or, _land_
> - <sup>k</sup> 23. Or, _which ascendeth_

## Chapter 75

### God abases the proud, but exalts the righteous.

<sup>1</sup> > _For the Chief Musician; _set to_ Al-tashheth. A Psalm of Asaph, a Song._We give thanks unto thee, O God;<br>We give thanks, for thy name is near:<br>Men tell of thy wondrous works.<sup>a</sup><br>
<sup>2</sup> When I shall <sup>b</sup>find the set time,<br>I will judge uprightly.<br>
<sup>3</sup> <sup>c</sup>The earth and all the inhabitants thereof are dissolved:<br>I have <sup>d</sup>set up the pillars of it. _[Selah_<br>
<sup>4</sup> I said unto the <sup>e</sup>arrogant, Deal not arrogantly;<br>And to the wicked, Lift not up the horn:<br>
<sup>5</sup> Lift not up your horn on high;<br>Speak not <sup>f</sup>with a stiff neck.<br>
<sup>6</sup> For neither from the east, nor from the west,<br>Nor yet <sup>g</sup>from the <sup>h</sup>south, _cometh_ lifting up.<br>
<sup>7</sup> But God is the judge:<br>He putteth down one, and lifteth up another.<br>
<sup>8</sup> For in the hand of Jehovah there is a cup, and the wine <sup>i</sup>foameth;<br>It is full of mixture, and he poureth out of the same:<br>Surely the dregs thereof, all the wicked of the earth shall drain them, and drink them.<br>
<sup>9</sup> But I will declare for ever,<br>I will sing praises to the God of Jacob.<br>
<sup>10</sup> All the horns of the wicked also will I cut off;<br>But the horns of the righteous shall be lifted up.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 1. Or, _for_ that _thy name is near Thy wondrous works declare_
> - <sup>b</sup> 2. Hebrew _take_.
> - <sup>c</sup> 3. Or, _When the earth . . . I set up_
> - <sup>d</sup> 3. Hebrew _proportioned_.
> - <sup>e</sup> 4. Or, _fools_
> - <sup>f</sup> 5. Or, _insolently with a_ haughty _neck_
> - <sup>g</sup> 6. Or, _from the wilderness of mountains_ cometh judgment
> - <sup>h</sup> 6. Hebrew _wilderness_.
> - <sup>i</sup> 8. Or, _is red_

## Chapter 76

### The victorious power of the God of Jacob.

<sup>1</sup> > _For the Chief Musician; on stringed instruments. A Psalm of Asaph, a Song._In Judah is God known:<br>His name is great in Israel.<br>
<sup>2</sup> In Salem also is his <sup>a</sup>tabernacle,<br>And his <sup>b</sup>dwelling-place in Zion.<br>
<sup>3</sup> There he brake the <sup>c</sup>arrows of the bow;<br>The shield, and the sword, and the battle. _[Selah_<br>
<sup>4</sup> Glorious art thou _and_ excellent,<sup>d</sup><br>From the mountains of prey.<br>
<sup>5</sup> The stouthearted are made a spoil,<br>They have slept their sleep;<br>And none of the men of might have found their hands.<br>
<sup>6</sup> At thy rebuke, O God of Jacob,<br>Both chariot and horse are cast into a dead sleep.<br>
<sup>7</sup> Thou, even thou, art to be feared;<br>And who may stand in thy sight when once thou art angry?<br>
<sup>8</sup> Thou didst cause sentence to be heard from heaven;<br>The earth feared, and was still,<br>
<sup>9</sup> When God arose to judgment,<br>To save all the meek of the earth. _[Selah_<br>
<sup>10</sup> Surely the wrath of man shall praise thee:<br>The residue of wrath shalt thou <sup>e</sup>gird upon thee.<br>
<sup>11</sup> Vow, and pay unto Jehovah your God:<br>Let all that are round about him bring presents unto him that ought to be feared.<br>
<sup>12</sup> He will cut off the spirit of princes:<br>He is terrible to the kings of the earth.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 2. Or, _covert_
> - <sup>b</sup> 2. Or, _lair_
> - <sup>c</sup> 3. Or, _fiery shafts_. Or, _lightnings_
> - <sup>d</sup> 4. Or, _More than_
> - <sup>e</sup> 10. Or, _restrain_

## Chapter 77

### Comfort in trouble from recalling God’s mighty deeds.

<sup>1</sup> > _For the Chief Musician; after the manner of Jeduthun. A Psalm of Asaph._I will cry unto God with my voice,<br>Even unto God with my voice; and he will give ear unto me.<br>
<sup>2</sup> In the day of my trouble I sought the Lord:<br>My hand was stretched out in the night, and slacked not;<br>My soul refused to be comforted.<br>
<sup>3</sup> I remember God, and am disquieted:<br>I complain, and my spirit <sup>a</sup>is overwhelmed. _[Selah_<br>
<sup>4</sup> Thou holdest mine eyes watching:<br>I am so troubled that I cannot speak.<br>
<sup>5</sup> I have considered the days of old,<br>The years of ancient times.<br>
<sup>6</sup> I call to remembrance my song in the night:<br>I commune with mine own heart;<br>And my spirit maketh diligent search.<br>
<sup>7</sup> Will the Lord cast off for ever?<br>And will he be favorable no more?<br>
<sup>8</sup> Is his lovingkindness clean gone for ever?<br>Doth his promise fail for evermore?<br>
<sup>9</sup> Hath God forgotten to be gracious?<br>Hath he in anger shut up his tender mercies? _[Selah_<br>
<sup>10</sup> And I said, <sup>b</sup>This is my infirmity;<br>_But I will remember_ the years of the right hand of the Most High.<br>
<sup>11</sup> I will make mention of the deeds of <sup>c</sup>Jehovah;<br>For I will remember thy wonders of old.<br>
<sup>12</sup> I will meditate also upon all thy work,<br>And muse on thy doings.<br>
<sup>13</sup> Thy way, O God, is <sup>d</sup>in the sanctuary:<br>Who is a great god like unto God?<br>
<sup>14</sup> Thou art the God that doest wonders:<br>Thou hast made known thy strength among the peoples.<br>
<sup>15</sup> Thou hast with thine arm redeemed thy people,<br>The sons of Jacob and Joseph. _[Selah_<br>
<sup>16</sup> The waters saw thee, O God;<br>The waters saw thee, they <sup>e</sup>were afraid:<br>The depths also trembled.<br>
<sup>17</sup> The clouds poured out water;<br>The skies sent out a sound:<br>Thine arrows also went abroad.<br>
<sup>18</sup> The voice of thy thunder was in the whirlwind;<br>The lightnings lightened the world:<br>The earth trembled and shook.<br>
<sup>19</sup> Thy way was in the sea,<br>And thy paths in the great waters,<br>And thy footsteps were not known.<br>
<sup>20</sup> Thou leddest thy people like a flock,<br>By the hand of Moses and Aaron.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 3. Or, _fainteth_
> - <sup>b</sup> 10. Or, _This is my grief, That the right hand of the Most High doth change_
> - <sup>c</sup> 11. Hebrew _Jah_.
> - <sup>d</sup> 13. Or, _in holiness_
> - <sup>e</sup> 16. Or, _were in pain_

## Chapter 78

### God’s guidance of his people in spite of their unfaithfulness.

<sup>1</sup> > _Maschil of Asaph._Give ear, O my people, to my law:<br>Incline your ears to the words of my mouth.<sup>a</sup><br>
<sup>2</sup> I will open my mouth in a parable;<br>I will utter dark sayings of old,<br>
<sup>3</sup> Which we have heard and known,<br>And our fathers have told us.<br>
<sup>4</sup> We will not hide them from their children,<br>Telling to the generation to come the praises of Jehovah,<br>And his strength, and his wondrous works that he hath done.<br>
<sup>5</sup> For he established a testimony in Jacob,<br>And appointed a law in Israel,<br>Which he commanded our fathers,<br>That they should make them known to their children;<br>
<sup>6</sup> That the generation to come might know _them_, even the children that should be born;<br>Who should arise and tell _them_ to their children,<br>
<sup>7</sup> That they might set their hope in God,<br>And not forget the works of God,<br>But keep his commandments,<br>
<sup>8</sup> And might not be as their fathers,<br>A stubborn and rebellious generation,<br>A generation <sup>b</sup>that set not their heart aright,<br>And whose spirit was not stedfast with God.<br>
<sup>9</sup> The children of Ephraim, being armed and carrying bows,<br>Turned back in the day of battle.<br>
<sup>10</sup> They kept not the covenant of God,<br>And refused to walk in his law;<br>
<sup>11</sup> And they forgat his doings,<br>And his wondrous works that he had showed them.<br>
<sup>12</sup> Marvellous things did he in the sight of their fathers,<br>In the land of Egypt, in the field of Zoan.<br>
<sup>13</sup> He clave the sea, and caused them to pass through;<br>And he made the waters to stand as a heap.<br>
<sup>14</sup> In the day-time also he led them with a cloud,<br>And all the night with a light of fire.<br>
<sup>15</sup> He clave rocks in the wilderness,<br>And gave them drink abundantly as out of the depths.<br>
<sup>16</sup> He brought streams also out of the rock,<br>And caused waters to run down like rivers.<br>
<sup>17</sup> Yet went they on still to sin against him,<br>To rebel against the Most High in <sup>c</sup>the desert.<br>
<sup>18</sup> And they tempted God in their heart<br>By asking food <sup>d</sup>according to their desire.<br>
<sup>19</sup> Yea, they spake against God;<br>They said, Can God prepare a table in the wilderness?<br>
<sup>20</sup> Behold, he smote the rock, so that waters gushed out,<br>And streams overflowed;<br>Can he give bread also?<br>Will he provide flesh for his people?<br>
<sup>21</sup> Therefore Jehovah heard, and was wroth;<br>And a fire was kindled against Jacob,<br>And anger also went up against Israel;<br>
<sup>22</sup> Because they believed not in God,<br>And trusted not in his salvation.<br>
<sup>23</sup> Yet he commanded the skies above,<br>And opened the doors of heaven;<br>
<sup>24</sup> And he rained down manna upon them to eat,<br>And gave them <sup>e</sup>food from heaven.<br>
<sup>25</sup> <sup>f</sup>Man did eat the bread of the mighty:<br>He sent them food to the full.<br>
<sup>26</sup> He <sup>g</sup>caused the east wind to blow in the heavens;<br>And by his power he guided the south wind.<br>
<sup>27</sup> He rained flesh also upon them as the dust,<br>And winged birds as the sand of the seas:<br>
<sup>28</sup> And he let it fall in the midst of their camp,<br>Round about their habitations.<br>
<sup>29</sup> So they did eat, and were well filled;<br>And he gave them their own desire.<br>
<sup>30</sup> They were not estranged from that which they desired,<br>Their food was yet in their mouths,<br>
<sup>31</sup> When the anger of God went up against them,<br>And slew of the fattest of them,<br>And smote down the young men of Israel.<br>
<sup>32</sup> For all this they sinned still,<br>And believed not in his wondrous works.<br>
<sup>33</sup> Therefore their days did he consume in vanity,<br>And their years in terror.<br>
<sup>34</sup> When he slew them, then they inquired after him;<br>And they returned and sought God earnestly.<br>
<sup>35</sup> And they remembered that God was their rock,<br>And the Most High God their redeemer.<br>
<sup>36</sup> But they flattered him with their mouth,<br>And lied unto him with their tongue.<br>
<sup>37</sup> For their heart was not <sup>h</sup>right with him,<br>Neither were they faithful in his covenant.<br>
<sup>38</sup> But he, being merciful, forgave _their_ iniquity, and destroyed _them_ not:<br>Yea, many a time turned he his anger away,<br>And did not stir up all his wrath.<br>
<sup>39</sup> And he remembered that they were but flesh,<br>A wind that passeth away, and cometh not again.<br>
<sup>40</sup> How oft did they rebel against him in the wilderness,<br>And grieve him in the desert!<br>
<sup>41</sup> And they turned again and tempted God,<br>And <sup>i</sup>provoked the Holy One of Israel.<br>
<sup>42</sup> They remembered not his hand,<br>Nor the day when he redeemed them from the adversary;<br>
<sup>43</sup> How he set his signs in Egypt,<br>And his wonders in the field of Zoan,<br>
<sup>44</sup> And turned their rivers into blood,<br>And their streams, so that they could not drink.<br>
<sup>45</sup> He sent among them swarms of flies, which devoured them;<br>And frogs, which destroyed them.<br>
<sup>46</sup> He gave also their increase unto the caterpillar,<br>And their labor unto the locust.<br>
<sup>47</sup> He <sup>j</sup>destroyed their vines with hail,<br>And their sycomore-trees with <sup>k</sup>frost.<br>
<sup>48</sup> He gave over their cattle also to the hail,<br>And their flocks to hot thunderbolts.<br>
<sup>49</sup> He cast upon them the fierceness of his anger,<br>Wrath, and indignation, and trouble,<sup>l</sup><br>A band of angels of evil.<br>
<sup>50</sup> He <sup>m</sup>made a path for his anger;<br>He spared not their soul from death,<br>But gave <sup>n</sup>their life over to the pestilence,<br>
<sup>51</sup> And smote all the first-born in Egypt,<br>The <sup>o</sup>chief of their strength in the tents of Ham.<br>
<sup>52</sup> But he led forth his own people like sheep,<br>And guided them in the wilderness like a flock.<br>
<sup>53</sup> And he led them safely, so that they feared not;<br>But the sea overwhelmed their enemies.<br>
<sup>54</sup> And he brought them to <sup>p</sup>the border of his sanctuary,<br>To this <sup>q</sup>mountain, which his right hand had gotten.<br>
<sup>55</sup> He drove out the nations also before them,<br>And allotted them for an inheritance by line,<br>And made the tribes of Israel to dwell in their tents.<br>
<sup>56</sup> Yet they tempted and rebelled against the Most High God,<br>And kept not his testimonies;<br>
<sup>57</sup> But turned back, and dealt treacherously like their fathers:<br>They were turned aside like a deceitful bow.<br>
<sup>58</sup> For they provoked him to anger with their high places,<br>And moved him to jealousy with their graven images.<br>
<sup>59</sup> When God heard _this_, he was wroth,<br>And greatly abhorred Israel;<br>
<sup>60</sup> So that he forsook the tabernacle of Shiloh,<br>The tent which he placed among men;<br>
<sup>61</sup> And delivered his strength into captivity,<br>And his glory into the adversary’s hand.<br>
<sup>62</sup> He gave his people over also unto the sword,<br>And was wroth with his inheritance.<br>
<sup>63</sup> Fire devoured their young men;<br>And their virgins had no marriage-song.<br>
<sup>64</sup> Their priests fell by the sword;<br>And their widows made no lamentation.<br>
<sup>65</sup> Then the Lord awaked as one out of sleep,<br>Like a mighty man that shouteth by reason of wine.<br>
<sup>66</sup> And he smote his adversaries backward:<br>He put them to a perpetual reproach.<br>
<sup>67</sup> Moreover he refused the tent of Joseph,<br>And chose not the tribe of Ephraim,<br>
<sup>68</sup> But chose the tribe of Judah,<br>The mount Zion which he loved.<br>
<sup>69</sup> And he built his sanctuary like the heights,<br>Like the earth which he hath established for ever.<br>
<sup>70</sup> He chose David also his servant,<br>And took him from the sheepfolds:<br>
<sup>71</sup> From following the ewes that have their young he brought him,<br>To be the shepherd of Jacob his people, and Israel his inheritance.<br>
<sup>72</sup> So he was their shepherd according to the integrity of his heart,<br>And guided them by the skilfulness of his hands.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 1. Or, _teaching_
> - <sup>b</sup> 8. Or, _that prepared not their heart_
> - <sup>c</sup> 17. Or, _a dry land_
> - <sup>d</sup> 18. Or, _for themselves_
> - <sup>e</sup> 24. Hebrew _grain_.
> - <sup>f</sup> 25. Or, _Every one_
> - <sup>g</sup> 26. Hebrew _led forth the east wind_.
> - <sup>h</sup> 37. Or, _stedfast_
> - <sup>i</sup> 41. Or, _limited_
> - <sup>j</sup> 47. Hebrew _killed_.
> - <sup>k</sup> 47. Or, _great hailstones_
> - <sup>l</sup> 49. Hebrew _A sending_.
> - <sup>m</sup> 50. Hebrew _levelled_.
> - <sup>n</sup> 50. Or, _their beasts to the murrain_
> - <sup>o</sup> 51. Hebrew _beginning_. See Dt. 21:17.
> - <sup>p</sup> 54. Or, _his holy border_
> - <sup>q</sup> 54. Or, _mountain land_

## Chapter 79

### A lament over the destruction of Jerusalem, and prayer for help.

<sup>1</sup> > _A Psalm of Asaph._O God, the nations are come into thine inheritance;<br>Thy holy temple have they defiled;<br>They have laid Jerusalem in heaps.<br>
<sup>2</sup> The dead bodies of thy servants have they given to be food unto the birds of the heavens,<br>The flesh of thy saints unto the beasts of the earth.<br>
<sup>3</sup> Their blood have they shed like water round about Jerusalem;<br>And there was none to bury them.<br>
<sup>4</sup> We are become a reproach to our neighbors,<br>A scoffing and derision to them that are round about us.<br>
<sup>5</sup> How long, O Jehovah? wilt thou be angry for ever?<br>Shall thy jealousy burn like fire?<br>
<sup>6</sup> Pour out thy wrath upon the nations that know thee not,<br>And upon the kingdoms that call not upon thy name.<br>
<sup>7</sup> For they have devoured Jacob,<br>And laid waste his <sup>a</sup>habitation.<br>
<sup>8</sup> Remember not against us the iniquities of our forefathers:<br>Let thy tender mercies speedily meet us;<br>For we are brought very low.<br>
<sup>9</sup> Help us, O God of our salvation, for the glory of thy name;<br>And deliver us, and <sup>b</sup>forgive our sins, for thy name’s sake.<br>
<sup>10</sup> Wherefore should the nations say, Where is their God?<br>Let the avenging of the blood of thy servants which is shed<br>Be known among the nations in our sight.<br>
<sup>11</sup> Let the sighing of the prisoner come before thee:<br>According to the greatness of <sup>c</sup>thy power preserve thou <sup>d</sup>those that are appointed to death;<br>
<sup>12</sup> And render unto our neighbors sevenfold into their bosom<br>Their reproach, wherewith they have reproached thee, O Lord.<br>
<sup>13</sup> So we thy people and sheep of thy pasture<br>Will give thee thanks for ever:<br>We will show forth thy praise to all generations.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 7. Or, _pasture_
> - <sup>b</sup> 9. Or, _expiate_
> - <sup>c</sup> 11. Hebrew _thine arm_.
> - <sup>d</sup> 11. Hebrew _the children of death_.

## Chapter 80

### God implored to rescue his people from their calamities.

<sup>1</sup> > _For the Chief Musician; set to Shoshannim Eduth. A Psalm of Asaph._Give ear, O Shepherd of Israel,<br>Thou that leadest Joseph like a flock;<br>Thou that sittest <sup>a</sup>_above_ the cherubim, shine forth.<sup>b</sup><br>
<sup>2</sup> Before Ephraim and Benjamin and Manasseh, stir up thy might,<br>And come to save us.<br>
<sup>3</sup> <sup>c</sup>Turn us again, O God;<br>And cause thy face to shine, and we shall be saved.<br>
<sup>4</sup> O Jehovah God of hosts,<br>How long <sup>d</sup>wilt thou be angry against the prayer of thy people?<br>
<sup>5</sup> Thou hast fed them with the bread of tears,<br>And given them tears to drink in large measure.<br>
<sup>6</sup> Thou makest us a strife unto our neighbors;<br>And our enemies laugh among themselves.<br>
<sup>7</sup> <sup>e</sup>Turn us again, O God of hosts;<br>And cause thy face to shine, and we shall be saved.<br>
<sup>8</sup> Thou broughtest a vine out of Egypt:<br>Thou didst drive out the nations, and plantedst it.<br>
<sup>9</sup> Thou preparedst _room_ before it,<br>And it took deep root, and filled the land.<br>
<sup>10</sup> The mountains were covered with the shadow of it,<br>And <sup>f</sup>the boughs thereof were _like_<sup>g</sup> cedars of God.<br>
<sup>11</sup> It sent out its branches unto the sea,<br>And its shoots unto the River.<br>
<sup>12</sup> Why hast thou broken down its walls,<br>So that all they that pass by the way do pluck it?<br>
<sup>13</sup> The boar out of the wood doth ravage it,<br>And the wild beasts of the field feed on it.<br>
<sup>14</sup> Turn again, we beseech thee, O God of hosts:<br>Look down from heaven, and behold, and visit this vine,<br>
<sup>15</sup> And <sup>h</sup>the stock which thy right hand planted,<br>And the <sup>i</sup>branch that thou madest strong for thyself.<br>
<sup>16</sup> It is burned with fire, it is cut down:<br>They perish at the rebuke of thy countenance.<br>
<sup>17</sup> Let thy hand be upon the man of thy right hand,<br>Upon the son of man whom thou madest strong for thyself.<br>
<sup>18</sup> So shall we not go back from thee:<br>Quicken thou us, and we will call upon thy name.<br>
<sup>19</sup> <sup>j</sup>Turn us again, O Jehovah God of hosts;<br>Cause thy face to shine, and we shall be saved.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 1. That is, _Lilies, a testimony_.
> - <sup>b</sup> 1. Or, _art enthroned_
> - <sup>c</sup> 3. Or, _Restore_
> - <sup>d</sup> 4. Hebrew _wilt thou smoke_. See 74:1.
> - <sup>e</sup> 7. Or, _Restore_
> - <sup>f</sup> 10. Or, _the cedars of God with the boughs thereof_
> - <sup>g</sup> 10. Or, _goodly cedars_
> - <sup>h</sup> 15. Or, _protect_ (or _maintain_) _that which etc._
> - <sup>i</sup> 15. Hebrew _son_.
> - <sup>j</sup> 19. Or, _Restore_

## Chapter 81

### God’s goodness and Israel’s waywardness.

<sup>1</sup> > _For the Chief Musician; set to the Gittith. _A Psalm_ of Asaph._Sing aloud unto God our strength:<br>Make a joyful noise unto the God of Jacob.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Raise a song, and <sup>a</sup>bring hither the timbrel,<br>The pleasant harp with the psaltery.<br>
<sup>3</sup> Blow the trumpet at the new moon,<br>At the full moon, on our feast-day.<br>
<sup>4</sup> For it is a statute for Israel,<br>An ordinance of the God of Jacob.<br>
<sup>5</sup> He appointed it in Joseph for a testimony,<br>When he went out <sup>b</sup>over the land of Egypt,<br>_Where_ I heard <sup>c</sup>a language that I knew not.<br>
<sup>6</sup> I removed his shoulder from the burden:<br>His hands were freed from the basket.<br>
<sup>7</sup> Thou calledst in trouble, and I delivered thee;<br>I answered thee in the secret place of thunder;<br>I proved thee at the waters of Meribah. _[Selah_<br>
<sup>8</sup> Hear, O my people, and I will testify unto thee:<br>O Israel, if thou wouldest hearken unto me!<br>
<sup>9</sup> There shall no strange god be in thee;<br>Neither shalt thou worship any foreign god.<br>
<sup>10</sup> I am Jehovah thy God,<br>Who brought thee up out of the land of Egypt:<br>Open thy mouth wide, and I will fill it.<br>
<sup>11</sup> But my people hearkened not to my voice;<br>And Israel would none of me.<br>
<sup>12</sup> So I let them go after the stubbornness of their heart,<br>That they might walk in their own counsels.<br>
<sup>13</sup> Oh that my people would hearken unto me,<br>That Israel would walk in my ways!<br>
<sup>14</sup> I would soon subdue their enemies,<br>And turn my hand against their adversaries.<br>
<sup>15</sup> The haters of Jehovah should <sup>d</sup>submit themselves unto him:<br>But their time should endure for ever.<br>
<sup>16</sup> He would feed them also with the <sup>e</sup>finest of the wheat;<br>And with honey out of the rock would I satisfy thee.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 2. Or, _strike the timbrel_
> - <sup>b</sup> 5. Or, _against_
> - <sup>c</sup> 5. Or, _the speech of one that etc._
> - <sup>d</sup> 15. Or, _yield feigned obedience_. Hebrew _lie_.
> - <sup>e</sup> 16. Hebrew _fat of wheat_.

## Chapter 82

### Unjust judgments rebuked.

<sup>1</sup> > _A Psalm of Asaph._God standeth in the congregation of God;<br>He judgeth among the gods.<br>
<sup>2</sup> How long will ye judge unjustly,<br>And respect the persons of the wicked? _[Selah_<br>
<sup>3</sup> Judge the <sup>a</sup>poor and fatherless:<br>Do justice to the afflicted and destitute.<br>
<sup>4</sup> Rescue the <sup>b</sup>poor and needy:<br>Deliver them out of the hand of the wicked.<br>
<sup>5</sup> They know not, neither do they understand;<br>They walk to and fro in darkness:<br>All the foundations of the earth are shaken.<br>
<sup>6</sup> I said, Ye are gods,<br>And all of you sons of the Most High.<br>
<sup>7</sup> Nevertheless ye shall die like men,<br>And fall like one of the princes.<br>
<sup>8</sup> Arise, O God, judge the earth;<br>For thou shalt inherit all the nations.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 3. Or, _weak_
> - <sup>b</sup> 4. Or, _weak_

## Chapter 83

### God implored to confound his enemies.

<sup>1</sup> > _A Song, a Psalm of Asaph._O God, keep not thou silence:<br>Hold not thy peace, and be not still, O God.<br>
<sup>2</sup> For, lo, thine enemies make a tumult;<br>And they that hate thee have lifted up the head.<br>
<sup>3</sup> They take crafty counsel against thy people,<br>And consult together against thy hidden ones.<br>
<sup>4</sup> They have said, Come, and let us cut them off from being a nation;<br>That the name of Israel may be no more in remembrance.<br>
<sup>5</sup> For they have consulted together with one consent;<br>Against thee do they make a covenant:<br>
<sup>6</sup> The tents of Edom and the Ishmaelites;<br>Moab, and the <sup>a</sup>Hagarenes;<br>
<sup>7</sup> Gebal, and Ammon, and Amalek;<br>Philistia with the inhabitants of Tyre:<br>
<sup>8</sup> Assyria also is joined with them;<br>They have <sup>b</sup>helped the children of Lot. _[Selah_<br>
<sup>9</sup> Do thou unto them as unto Midian,<br>As to Sisera, as to Jabin, at the river Kishon;<br>
<sup>10</sup> Who perished at Endor,<br>Who became as dung for the earth.<br>
<sup>11</sup> Make their nobles like Oreb and Zeeb;<br>Yea, all their princes like Zebah and Zalmunna;<br>
<sup>12</sup> Who said, Let us take to ourselves in possession<br>The <sup>c</sup>habitations of God.<br>
<sup>13</sup> O my God, make them like the whirling dust;<br>As stubble before the wind.<br>
<sup>14</sup> As the fire that burneth the forest,<br>And as the flame that setteth the mountains on fire,<br>
<sup>15</sup> So pursue them with thy tempest,<br>And terrify them with thy storm.<br>
<sup>16</sup> Fill their faces with confusion,<br>That they may seek thy name, O Jehovah.<br>
<sup>17</sup> Let them be put to shame and dismayed for ever;<br>Yea, let them be confounded and perish;<br>
<sup>18</sup> That they may know that <sup>d</sup>thou alone, whose name is Jehovah,<br>Art the Most High over all the earth.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 6. Or, _Hagrites_. See 1 Chr. 5:10.
> - <sup>b</sup> 8. Hebrew _been an arm to the children of Lot_.
> - <sup>c</sup> 12. Or, _pastures_
> - <sup>d</sup> 18. Or, _thou, whose name alone is Jehovah, art etc._

## Chapter 84

### Longing for the Temple worship.

<sup>1</sup> > _For the Chief Musician; set to the Gittith. A Psalm of the sons of Korah._How amiable are thy tabernacles,<br>O Jehovah of hosts!<sup>a</sup><br>
<sup>2</sup> My soul longeth, yea, even fainteth for the courts of Jehovah;<br>My heart and my flesh <sup>b</sup>cry out unto the living God.<br>
<sup>3</sup> Yea, the sparrow hath found her a house,<br>And the swallow a nest for herself, where she may lay her young,<br>Even thine altars, O Jehovah of hosts,<br>My King, and my God.<br>
<sup>4</sup> Blessed are they that dwell in thy house:<br>They will be still praising thee. _[Selah_<br>
<sup>5</sup> Blessed is the man whose strength is in thee;<br>In whose heart are the highways _to Zion_.<br>
<sup>6</sup> Passing through the valley of <sup>c</sup>Weeping they make it a place of springs;<br>Yea, the early rain covereth it with blessings.<br>
<sup>7</sup> They go from strength to strength;<br>Every one of them appeareth before God in Zion.<br>
<sup>8</sup> O Jehovah God of hosts, hear my prayer;<br>Give ear, O God of Jacob. _[Selah_<br>
<sup>9</sup> <sup>d</sup>Behold, O God our shield,<br>And look upon the face of thine anointed.<br>
<sup>10</sup> For a day in thy courts is better than a thousand.<br>I had rather <sup>e</sup>be a doorkeeper in the house of my God,<br>Than to dwell in the tents of wickedness.<br>
<sup>11</sup> For Jehovah God is a sun and a shield:<br>Jehovah will give grace and glory;<br>No good thing will he withhold from them that walk uprightly.<br>
<sup>12</sup> O Jehovah of hosts,<br>Blessed is the man that trusteth in thee.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 1. Or, _lovely_
> - <sup>b</sup> 2. Or, _sing for joy_
> - <sup>c</sup> 6. Or, _balsam-trees_. Hebrew _Baca_. See 2 Sam. 5:23.
> - <sup>d</sup> 9. Or, _Behold our shield, O God_
> - <sup>e</sup> 10. Or, _stand at the threshold of etc._

## Chapter 85

### Prayer for God’s mercy upon the nation.

<sup>1</sup> > _For the Chief Musician. A Psalm of the sons of Korah._Jehovah, thou hast been favorable unto thy land;<br>Thou hast brought back the captivity of Jacob.<sup>a</sup><br>
<sup>2</sup> Thou hast forgiven the iniquity of thy people;<br>Thou hast covered all their sin. _[Selah_<br>
<sup>3</sup> Thou hast taken away all thy wrath;<br>Thou hast turned _thyself_ from the fierceness of thine anger.<br>
<sup>4</sup> <sup>b</sup>Turn us, O God of our salvation,<br>And cause thine indignation toward us to cease.<br>
<sup>5</sup> Wilt thou be angry with us for ever?<br>Wilt thou draw out thine anger to all generations?<br>
<sup>6</sup> Wilt thou not quicken us again,<br>That thy people may rejoice in thee?<br>
<sup>7</sup> Show us thy lovingkindness, O Jehovah,<br>And grant us thy salvation.<br>
<sup>8</sup> I will hear what God Jehovah will speak;<br>For he will speak peace unto his people, and to his saints:<br>But let them not turn again to folly.<br>
<sup>9</sup> Surely his salvation is nigh them that fear him,<br>That glory may dwell in our land.<br>
<sup>10</sup> Mercy and truth are met together;<br>Righteousness and peace have kissed each other.<br>
<sup>11</sup> Truth springeth out of the earth;<br>And righteousness hath looked down from heaven.<br>
<sup>12</sup> Yea, Jehovah will give that which is good;<br>And our land shall yield its increase.<br>
<sup>13</sup> Righteousness shall go before him,<br>And shall <sup>c</sup>make his footsteps a way _to walk in_.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 1. Or, _returned to_
> - <sup>b</sup> 4. Or, _Turn to us_
> - <sup>c</sup> 13. Or, _set_ us _in the way of his steps_

## Chapter 86

### A psalm of supplication and trust.

<sup>1</sup> > _A Prayer of David._Bow down thine ear, O Jehovah, and answer me;<br>For I am poor and needy.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Preserve my soul; for I am godly:<br>O thou my God, save thy servant that trusteth in thee.<br>
<sup>3</sup> Be merciful unto me, O Lord;<br>For unto thee do I cry all the day long.<br>
<sup>4</sup> Rejoice the soul of thy servant;<br>For unto thee, O Lord, do I lift up my soul.<br>
<sup>5</sup> For thou, Lord, art good, and ready to forgive,<br>And abundant in lovingkindness unto all them that call upon thee.<br>
<sup>6</sup> Give ear, O Jehovah, unto my prayer;<br>And hearken unto the voice of my supplications.<br>
<sup>7</sup> In the day of my trouble I will call upon thee;<br>For thou wilt answer me.<br>
<sup>8</sup> There is none like unto thee among the gods, O Lord;<br>Neither _are there any works_ like unto thy works.<br>
<sup>9</sup> All nations whom thou hast made shall come and worship before thee, O Lord;<br>And they shall glorify thy name.<br>
<sup>10</sup> For thou art great, and doest wondrous things:<br>Thou art God alone.<br>
<sup>11</sup> Teach me thy way, O Jehovah; I will walk in thy truth:<br>Unite my heart to fear thy name.<br>
<sup>12</sup> I will praise thee, O Lord my God, with my whole heart;<br>And I will glorify thy name for evermore.<br>
<sup>13</sup> For great is thy lovingkindness toward me;<br>And thou hast delivered my soul from the lowest Sheol.<br>
<sup>14</sup> O God, the proud are risen up against me,<br>And a company of violent men have sought after my soul,<br>And have not set thee before them.<br>
<sup>15</sup> But thou, O Lord, art a God merciful and gracious,<br>Slow to anger, and abundant in lovingkindness and truth.<br>
<sup>16</sup> Oh turn unto me, and <sup>a</sup>have mercy upon me;<br>Give thy strength unto thy servant,<br>And save the son of thy handmaid.<br>
<sup>17</sup> Show me a token for good,<br>That they who hate me may see it, and be put to shame,<br>Because thou, Jehovah, hast helped me, and comforted me.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 16. Or, _be gracious unto_

## Chapter 87

### The privileges of citizenship in Zion.

<sup>1</sup> > _A Psalm of the sons of Korah; a Song._His foundation is in the holy mountains.<sup>a</sup><br>
<sup>2</sup> Jehovah loveth the gates of Zion<br>More than all the dwellings of Jacob.<br>
<sup>3</sup> Glorious things are spoken of thee,<br>O city of God. _[Selah_<br>
<sup>4</sup> I will make mention of <sup>b</sup>Rahab and Babylon as among them that know me:<br>Behold, Philistia, and Tyre, with <sup>c</sup>Ethiopia:<br>This one was born there.<br>
<sup>5</sup> Yea, of Zion it shall be said, This one and that one was born in her;<br>And the Most High himself will establish her.<br>
<sup>6</sup> Jehovah will count, when he writeth up the peoples,<br>This one was born there. _[Selah_<br>
<sup>7</sup> They that sing as well as <sup>d</sup>they that dance _shall say_,<br>All my fountains are in thee.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 1. Or, _His foundation in the holy mountains Jehovah loveth, even the gates etc._
> - <sup>b</sup> 4. Or, _Egypt_
> - <sup>c</sup> 4. Hebrew _Cush_.
> - <sup>d</sup> 7. Or, _the players on instruments_ shall be there

## Chapter 88

### A petition to be saved from death.

<sup>1</sup> > _A Song, a Psalm of the sons of Korah; for the Chief Musician; set to Mahalath Leannoth. Maschil of Heman the Ezrahite._O Jehovah, the God of my salvation,<br>I have cried day and night before thee.<sup>a</sup><br>
<sup>2</sup> Let my prayer enter into thy presence;<br>Incline thine ear unto my cry.<br>
<sup>3</sup> For my soul is full of troubles,<br>And my life draweth nigh unto Sheol.<br>
<sup>4</sup> I am reckoned with them that go down into the pit;<br>I am as a man that hath no help,<br>
<sup>5</sup> Cast <sup>b</sup>off among the dead,<br>Like the slain that lie in the grave,<br>Whom thou rememberest no more,<br>And they are cut off from thy hand.<br>
<sup>6</sup> Thou hast laid me in the lowest pit,<br>In dark places, in the deeps.<br>
<sup>7</sup> Thy wrath lieth hard upon me,<br>And thou hast afflicted me with all thy waves. _[Selah_<br>
<sup>8</sup> Thou hast put mine acquaintance far from me;<br>Thou hast made me an abomination unto them:<br>I am shut up, and I cannot come forth.<br>
<sup>9</sup> Mine eye wasteth away by reason of affliction:<br>I have called daily upon thee, O Jehovah;<br>I have spread forth my hands unto thee.<br>
<sup>10</sup> Wilt thou show wonders to the dead?<br>Shall <sup>c</sup>they that are deceased arise and praise thee? _[Selah_<br>
<sup>11</sup> Shall thy lovingkindness be declared in the grave?<br>Or thy faithfulness in <sup>d</sup>Destruction?<br>
<sup>12</sup> Shall thy wonders be known in the dark?<br>And thy righteousness in the land of forgetfulness?<br>
<sup>13</sup> But unto thee, O Jehovah, have I cried;<br>And in the morning shall my prayer come before thee.<br>
<sup>14</sup> Jehovah, why castest thou off my soul?<br>Why hidest thou thy face from me?<br>
<sup>15</sup> I am afflicted and ready to die from my youth up:<br>While I suffer thy terrors I am distracted.<br>
<sup>16</sup> Thy fierce wrath is gone over me;<br>Thy terrors have cut me off.<br>
<sup>17</sup> They came round about me like water all the day long;<br>They compassed me about together.<br>
<sup>18</sup> Lover and friend hast thou put far from me,<br>And mine acquaintance <sup>e</sup>into darkness.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 1. Or, _for singing_
> - <sup>b</sup> 5. Or, _away_
> - <sup>c</sup> 10. Or, _the shades_. Hebrew _Rephaim_.
> - <sup>d</sup> 11. Hebrew _Abaddon_. See Job 26:6.
> - <sup>e</sup> 18. Or, _are darkness_

## Chapter 89

### Jehovah’s covenant with David, and Israel’s afflictions.

<sup>1</sup> > _Maschil of Ethan the Ezrahite._I will sing of the lovingkindness of Jehovah for ever:<br>With my mouth will I make known thy faithfulness to all generations.<br>
<sup>2</sup> For I have said, Mercy shall be built up for ever;<br>Thy faithfulness wilt thou establish in the very heavens.<br>
<sup>3</sup> I have made a covenant with my chosen,<br>I have sworn unto David my servant:<br>
<sup>4</sup> Thy seed will I establish for ever,<br>And build up thy throne to all generations. _[Selah_<br>
<sup>5</sup> And the heavens shall praise thy wonders, O Jehovah;<br>Thy faithfulness also in the assembly of the holy ones.<br>
<sup>6</sup> For who in the skies can be compared unto Jehovah?<br>Who among the <sup>a</sup>sons of the <sup>b</sup>mighty is like unto Jehovah,<br>
<sup>7</sup> A God very terrible in the council of the holy ones,<br>And to be feared above all them that are round about him?<br>
<sup>8</sup> O Jehovah God of hosts,<br>Who is a mighty one, like unto thee, O <sup>c</sup>Jehovah?<br>And thy faithfulness is round about thee.<br>
<sup>9</sup> Thou rulest the pride of the sea:<br>When the waves thereof arise, thou stillest them.<br>
<sup>10</sup> Thou hast broken <sup>d</sup>Rahab in pieces, as one that is slain;<br>Thou hast scattered thine enemies with the arm of thy strength.<br>
<sup>11</sup> The heavens are thine, the earth also is thine:<br>The world and the fulness thereof, thou hast founded them.<br>
<sup>12</sup> The north and the south, thou hast created them:<br>Tabor and Hermon rejoice in thy name.<br>
<sup>13</sup> Thou hast <sup>e</sup>a mighty arm;<br>Strong is thy hand, and high is thy right hand.<br>
<sup>14</sup> Righteousness and justice are the foundation of thy throne:<br>Lovingkindness and truth go before thy face.<br>
<sup>15</sup> Blessed is the people that know the <sup>f</sup>joyful sound:<br>They walk, O Jehovah, in the light of thy countenance.<br>
<sup>16</sup> In thy name do they rejoice all the day;<br>And in thy righteousness are they exalted.<br>
<sup>17</sup> For thou art the glory of their strength;<br>And in thy favor <sup>g</sup>our horn shall be exalted.<br>
<sup>18</sup> For our shield belongeth unto Jehovah;<sup>h</sup><br>And our king to the Holy One of Israel.<br>
<sup>19</sup> Then thou spakest in vision to thy <sup>i</sup>saints,<br>And saidst, I have laid help upon one that is mighty;<br>I have exalted one chosen out of the people.<br>
<sup>20</sup> I have found David my servant;<br>With my holy oil have I anointed him:<br>
<sup>21</sup> With whom my hand shall be established;<br>Mine arm also shall strengthen him.<br>
<sup>22</sup> The enemy shall not <sup>j</sup>exact from him,<br>Nor the son of wickedness afflict him.<br>
<sup>23</sup> And I will beat down his adversaries before him,<br>And smite them that hate him.<br>
<sup>24</sup> But my faithfulness and my lovingkindness shall be with him;<br>And in my name shall his horn be exalted.<br>
<sup>25</sup> I will set his hand also on the sea,<br>And his right hand on the rivers.<br>
<sup>26</sup> He shall cry unto me, Thou art my Father,<br>My God, and the rock of my salvation.<br>
<sup>27</sup> I also will make him _my_ first-born,<br>The highest of the kings of the earth.<br>
<sup>28</sup> My lovingkindness will I keep for him for evermore;<br>And my covenant shall <sup>k</sup>stand fast with him.<br>
<sup>29</sup> His seed also will I make to endure for ever,<br>And his throne as the days of heaven.<br>
<sup>30</sup> If his children forsake my law,<br>And walk not in mine ordinances;<br>
<sup>31</sup> If they <sup>l</sup>break my statutes,<br>And keep not my commandments;<br>
<sup>32</sup> Then will I visit their transgression with the rod,<br>And their iniquity with stripes.<br>
<sup>33</sup> But my lovingkindness will I not utterly take from him,<br>Nor suffer my faithfulness to fail.<br>
<sup>34</sup> My covenant will I not <sup>m</sup>break,<br>Nor alter the thing that is gone out of my lips.<br>
<sup>35</sup> <sup>n</sup>Once have I sworn by my holiness:<br>I will not lie unto David:<br>
<sup>36</sup> His seed shall endure for ever,<br>And his throne as the sun before me.<br>
<sup>37</sup> <sup>o</sup>It shall be established for ever as the moon,<sup>p</sup><br>And _as_ the faithful witness in the sky. _[Selah_<br>
<sup>38</sup> But thou hast cast off and rejected,<br>Thou hast been wroth with thine anointed.<br>
<sup>39</sup> Thou hast abhorred the covenant of thy servant:<br>Thou hast profaned his crown _by casting it_ to the ground.<br>
<sup>40</sup> Thou hast broken down all his hedges;<br>Thou hast brought his strongholds to ruin.<br>
<sup>41</sup> All that pass by the way rob him:<br>He is become a reproach to his neighbors.<br>
<sup>42</sup> Thou hast exalted the right hand of his adversaries;<br>Thou hast made all his enemies to rejoice.<br>
<sup>43</sup> Yea, thou turnest back the edge of his sword,<br>And hast not made him to stand in the battle.<br>
<sup>44</sup> Thou hast made his brightness to cease,<br>And cast his throne down to the ground.<br>
<sup>45</sup> The days of his youth hast thou shortened:<br>Thou hast covered him with shame. _[Selah_<br>
<sup>46</sup> How long, O Jehovah? wilt thou hide thyself for ever?<br>_How long_ shall thy wrath burn like fire?<br>
<sup>47</sup> Oh remember how short my time is:<br>For what vanity hast thou created all the children of men!<br>
<sup>48</sup> What man is he that shall live and not see death,<br>That shall deliver his soul from the <sup>q</sup>power of Sheol? _[Selah_<br>
<sup>49</sup> Lord, where are thy former lovingkindnesses,<br>Which thou swarest unto David in thy faithfulness?<br>
<sup>50</sup> Remember, Lord, the reproach of thy servants;<br>How I do bear in my bosom _the reproach of_ all the <sup>r</sup>mighty peoples,<br>
<sup>51</sup> Wherewith thine enemies have reproached, O Jehovah,<br>Wherewith they have reproached the footsteps of thine anointed.<br>
<sup>52</sup> Blessed be Jehovah for evermore.<br>Amen, and Amen.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 6. Or, _sons of God_
> - <sup>b</sup> 6. Or, _gods_. See 29:1.
> - <sup>c</sup> 8. Hebrew _Jah_.
> - <sup>d</sup> 10. Or, _Egypt_
> - <sup>e</sup> 13. Hebrew _an arm with might_.
> - <sup>f</sup> 15. Or, _trumpet sound_
> - <sup>g</sup> 17. Another reading is, _thou shalt exalt our horn_.
> - <sup>h</sup> 18. Or, _Even to the Holy One of Israel our King_
> - <sup>i</sup> 19. Or, as otherwise read, _saint_
> - <sup>j</sup> 22. Or, _do him violence_
> - <sup>k</sup> 28. Or, _be faithful_
> - <sup>l</sup> 31. Hebrew _profane_.
> - <sup>m</sup> 34. Hebrew _profane_.
> - <sup>n</sup> 35. Or, _One thing_
> - <sup>o</sup> 37. Or, _As the moon which is established for ever_
> - <sup>p</sup> 37. Or, _And the witness in the sky is faithful_
> - <sup>q</sup> 48. Hebrew _hand_.
> - <sup>r</sup> 50. Or, _many_

## Chapter 90

### God’s eternity and man’s transitoriness.

<sup>1</sup> > _A Prayer of Moses the man of God._Lord, thou hast been our dwelling-place<br>In all generations.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Before the mountains were brought forth,<br>Or ever thou <sup>a</sup>hadst formed the earth and the world,<br>Even from everlasting to everlasting, thou art God.<br>
<sup>3</sup> Thou turnest man to <sup>b</sup>destruction,<br>And sayest, Return, ye children of men.<br>
<sup>4</sup> For a thousand years in thy sight<br>Are but as yesterday <sup>c</sup>when it is past,<br>And as a watch in the night.<br>
<sup>5</sup> Thou carriest them away as with a flood; they are as a sleep:<br>In the morning they are like grass which groweth up.<br>
<sup>6</sup> In the morning it flourisheth, and groweth up;<br>In the evening it is cut down, and withereth.<br>
<sup>7</sup> For we are consumed in thine anger,<br>And in thy wrath are we troubled.<br>
<sup>8</sup> Thou hast set our iniquities before thee,<br>Our secret sins in the light of thy countenance.<br>
<sup>9</sup> For all our days are passed away in thy wrath:<br>We bring our years to an end as <sup>d</sup>a sigh.<br>
<sup>10</sup> The days of our years are threescore years and ten,<br>Or even by reason of strength fourscore years;<br>Yet is their pride but labor and sorrow;<br>For it is soon gone, and we fly away.<br>
<sup>11</sup> Who knoweth the power of thine anger,<br>And thy wrath according to the fear that is due unto thee?<br>
<sup>12</sup> So teach us to number our days,<br>That we may get us a heart of wisdom.<br>
<sup>13</sup> Return, O Jehovah; how long?<br>And let it repent thee concerning thy servants.<br>
<sup>14</sup> Oh satisfy us in the morning with thy lovingkindness,<br>That we may rejoice and be glad all our days.<br>
<sup>15</sup> Make us glad according to the days wherein thou hast afflicted us,<br>And the years wherein we have seen evil.<br>
<sup>16</sup> Let thy work appear unto thy servants,<br>And thy glory upon their children.<br>
<sup>17</sup> And let the <sup>e</sup>favor of the Lord our God be upon us;<br>And establish thou the work of our hands upon us;<br>Yea, the work of our hands establish thou it.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 2. Hebrew _gavest birth to_.
> - <sup>b</sup> 3. Or, _dust_. Hebrew _crushing_.
> - <sup>c</sup> 4. Or, _when it passeth_
> - <sup>d</sup> 9. Or, _a sound_
> - <sup>e</sup> 17. Or, _beauty_

## Chapter 91

### Security of him who trusts in Jehovah.

<sup>1</sup> He that dwelleth in the secret place of the Most High<br>Shall abide under the shadow of the Alm<sup>a</sup>ighty.<br>
<sup>2</sup> I will say of Jehovah, He is my refuge and my fortress;<br>My God, in whom I trust.<br>
<sup>3</sup> For he will deliver thee from the snare of the fowler,<br>And from the deadly pestilence.<br>
<sup>4</sup> He will cover thee with his pinions,<br>And under his wings shalt thou take refuge:<br>His truth is a shield and a buckler.<br>
<sup>5</sup> Thou shalt not be afraid for the terror by night,<br>Nor for the arrow that flieth by day;<br>
<sup>6</sup> For the pestilence that walketh in darkness,<br>Nor for the destruction that wasteth at noonday.<br>
<sup>7</sup> A thousand shall fall at thy side,<br>And ten thousand at thy right hand;<br>_But_ it shall not come nigh thee.<br>
<sup>8</sup> Only with thine eyes shalt thou behold,<br>And see the reward of the wicked.<br>
<sup>9</sup> <sup>b</sup>For thou, O Jehovah, art my refuge!<br>Thou hast made the Most High thy habitation;<br>
<sup>10</sup> There shall no evil befall thee,<br>Neither shall any plague come nigh thy tent.<br>
<sup>11</sup> For he will give his angels charge over thee,<br>To keep thee in all thy ways.<br>
<sup>12</sup> They shall bear thee up in their hands,<br>Lest thou dash thy foot against a stone.<br>
<sup>13</sup> Thou shalt tread upon the lion and adder:<br>The young lion and the serpent shalt thou trample under foot.<br>
<sup>14</sup> Because he hath set his love upon me, therefore will I deliver him:<br>I will set him on high, because he hath known my name.<br>
<sup>15</sup> He shall call upon me, and I will answer him;<br>I will be with him in trouble:<br>I will deliver him, and honor him.<br>
<sup>16</sup> With long life will I satisfy him,<br>And show him my salvation.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 1. Or, _That abideth . . . Almighty; Even I etc._
> - <sup>b</sup> 9. Or, _Because thou_ hast said, _Jehovah is my refuge_.

## Chapter 92

### Praise for Jehovah’s goodness.

<sup>1</sup> > _A Psalm, a Song for the sabbath day._It is a good thing to give thanks unto Jehovah,<br>And to sing praises unto thy name, O Most High;<br>
<sup>2</sup> To show forth thy lovingkindness in the morning,<br>And thy faithfulness every night,<br>
<sup>3</sup> With an instrument of ten strings, and with the psaltery;<br>With a solemn sound upon the harp.<br>
<sup>4</sup> For thou, Jehovah, hast made me glad through thy work:<br>I will triumph in the works of thy hands.<br>
<sup>5</sup> How great are thy works, O Jehovah!<br>Thy thoughts are very deep.<br>
<sup>6</sup> A brutish man knoweth not;<br>Neither doth a fool understand this:<br>
<sup>7</sup> When the wicked spring as the grass,<br>And when all the workers of iniquity do flourish;<br>It is that they shall be destroyed for ever.<br>
<sup>8</sup> But thou, O Jehovah, art on high for evermore.<br>
<sup>9</sup> For, lo, thine enemies, O Jehovah,<br>For, lo, thine enemies shall perish;<br>All the workers of iniquity shall be scattered.<br>
<sup>10</sup> But my horn hast thou exalted like _the horn of_ the wild-ox:<br>I am anointed with fresh oil.<br>
<sup>11</sup> Mine eye also hath seen _my desire_ on <sup>a</sup>mine enemies,<br>Mine ears have heard _my desire_ of the evil-doers that rise up against me.<br>
<sup>12</sup> The righteous shall flourish like the palm-tree:<br>He shall grow like a cedar in Lebanon.<br>
<sup>13</sup> They are planted in the house of Jehovah;<br>They shall flourish in the courts of our God.<br>
<sup>14</sup> They shall still bring forth fruit in old age;<br>They shall be full of sap and green:<br>
<sup>15</sup> To show that Jehovah is upright;<br>He is my rock, and there is no unrighteousness in him.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 11. Or, _them that lie in wait for me_

## Chapter 93

### The majesty of Jehovah.

<sup>1</sup> Jehovah reigneth; he is clothed with majesty;<br>Jehovah is clothed with strength; he hath girded himself therewith:<br>The world also is established, that it cannot be moved.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Thy throne is established of old:<br>Thou art from everlasting.<br>
<sup>3</sup> The floods have lifted up, O Jehovah,<br>The floods have lifted up their voice;<br>The floods lift up their <sup>a</sup>waves.<br>
<sup>4</sup> Above the voices of many waters,<br>The mighty breakers of the sea,<br>Jehovah on high is mighty.<br>
<sup>5</sup> Thy testimonies are very sure:<br>Holiness becometh thy house,<br>O Jehovah, for evermore.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 3. Or, _roaring_

## Chapter 94

### Jehovah implored to avenge his people.

<sup>1</sup> O Jehovah, thou God to whom vengeance belongeth,<br>Thou God to whom vengeance belongeth, shine forth.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Lift up thyself, thou judge of the earth:<br>Render to the proud _their_ desert.<br>
<sup>3</sup> Jehovah, how long shall the wicked,<br>How long shall the wicked triumph?<br>
<sup>4</sup> They prate, they speak arrogantly:<br>All the workers of iniquity boast themselves.<br>
<sup>5</sup> They break in pieces thy people, O Jehovah,<br>And afflict thy heritage.<br>
<sup>6</sup> They slay the widow and the sojourner,<br>And murder the fatherless.<br>
<sup>7</sup> And they say, <sup>a</sup>Jehovah will not see,<br>Neither will the God of Jacob consider.<br>
<sup>8</sup> Consider, ye brutish among the people;<br>And ye fools, when will ye be wise?<br>
<sup>9</sup> He that planted the ear, shall he not hear?<br>He that formed the eye, shall he not see?<br>
<sup>10</sup> He that <sup>b</sup>chastiseth the nations, shall not he correct,<br>_Even_ he that teacheth man knowledge?<br>
<sup>11</sup> Jehovah knoweth the thoughts of man,<sup>c</sup><br>That they are <sup>d</sup>vanity.<br>
<sup>12</sup> Blessed is the man whom thou chastenest, O <sup>e</sup>Jehovah,<br>And teachest out of thy law;<br>
<sup>13</sup> That thou mayest give him rest from the days of adversity,<br>Until the pit be digged for the wicked.<br>
<sup>14</sup> For Jehovah will not cast off his people,<br>Neither will he forsake his inheritance.<br>
<sup>15</sup> For judgment shall return unto righteousness;<br>And all the upright in heart shall follow it.<br>
<sup>16</sup> Who will rise up for me against the evil-doers?<br>Who will stand up for me against the workers of iniquity?<br>
<sup>17</sup> Unless Jehovah had been my help,<br>My soul had soon dwelt in silence.<br>
<sup>18</sup> When I said, My foot slippeth;<br>Thy lovingkindness, O Jehovah, held me up.<br>
<sup>19</sup> In the multitude of my <sup>f</sup>thoughts within me<br>Thy comforts delight my soul.<br>
<sup>20</sup> Shall the <sup>g</sup>throne of wickedness have fellowship with thee,<br>Which frameth mischief by statute?<br>
<sup>21</sup> They gather themselves together against the soul of the righteous,<br>And condemn the innocent blood.<br>
<sup>22</sup> But Jehovah hath been my high tower,<br>And my God the rock of my refuge.<br>
<sup>23</sup> And he hath brought upon them their own iniquity,<br>And will cut them off in their own wickedness;<br>Jehovah our God will cut them off.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 7. Hebrew _Jah_.
> - <sup>b</sup> 10. Or, _instructeth_
> - <sup>c</sup> 11. Or, _For_
> - <sup>d</sup> 11. Hebrew _a breath_.
> - <sup>e</sup> 12. Hebrew _Jah_.
> - <sup>f</sup> 19. Or, _doubts_
> - <sup>g</sup> 20. Or, _seat_

## Chapter 95

### Praise to Jehovah, and warning against unbelief.

<sup>1</sup> Oh come, let us sing unto Jehovah;<br>Let us make a joyful noise to the rock of our salvation.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Let us come before his presence with thanksgiving;<br>Let us make a joyful noise unto him with psalms.<br>
<sup>3</sup> For Jehovah is a great God,<br>And a great King above all gods.<br>
<sup>4</sup> In his hand are the deep places of the earth;<br>The <sup>a</sup>heights of the mountains are his also.<br>
<sup>5</sup> The sea is his, and he made it;<br>And his hands formed the dry land.<br>
<sup>6</sup> Oh come, let us worship and bow down;<br>Let us kneel before Jehovah our Maker:<br>
<sup>7</sup> For he is our God,<br>And we are the people of his pasture, and the sheep of his hand.<br>To-day, <sup>b</sup>oh that ye would hear his voice!<br>
<sup>8</sup> Harden not your heart, as at <sup>c</sup>Meribah,<br>As in the day of <sup>d</sup>Massah in the wilderness;<br>
<sup>9</sup> When your fathers tempted me,<br>Proved me, and saw my work.<br>
<sup>10</sup> Forty years long was I grieved with _that_ generation,<br>And said, It is a people that do err in their heart,<br>And they have not known my ways:<br>
<sup>11</sup> Wherefore I sware in my wrath,<br>That they should not enter into my rest.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 4. Or, _strength_
> - <sup>b</sup> 7. Or, _if ye will hear his voice, Harden etc._
> - <sup>c</sup> 8. That is, _strife_.
> - <sup>d</sup> 8. That is, _temptation_.

## Chapter 96

### A call to worship Jehovah the Righteous Judge.

<sup>1</sup> Oh sing unto Jehovah a new song:<br>Sing unto Jehovah, all the earth.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Sing unto Jehovah, bless his name;<br>Show forth his salvation from day to day.<br>
<sup>3</sup> Declare his glory among the nations,<br>His marvellous works among all the peoples.<br>
<sup>4</sup> For great is Jehovah, and greatly to be praised:<br>He is to be feared above all gods.<br>
<sup>5</sup> For all the gods of the peoples are <sup>a</sup>idols;<br>But Jehovah made the heavens.<br>
<sup>6</sup> Honor and majesty are before him:<br>Strength and beauty are in his sanctuary.<br>
<sup>7</sup> Ascribe unto Jehovah, ye kindreds of the peoples,<br>Ascribe unto Jehovah glory and strength.<br>
<sup>8</sup> Ascribe unto Jehovah the glory due unto his name:<br>Bring an offering, and come into his courts.<br>
<sup>9</sup> Oh worship Jehovah <sup>b</sup>in holy array:<br>Tremble before him, all the earth.<br>
<sup>10</sup> Say among the nations, Jehovah reigneth:<br>The world also is established that it cannot be moved:<br>He will judge the peoples with equity.<br>
<sup>11</sup> Let the heavens be glad, and let the earth rejoice;<br>Let the sea roar, and the fulness thereof;<br>
<sup>12</sup> Let the field exult, and all that is therein;<br>Then shall all the trees of the wood sing for joy<br>
<sup>13</sup> Before Jehovah; for he cometh,<br>For he cometh to judge the earth:<br>He will judge the world with righteousness,<br>And the peoples <sup>c</sup>with his truth.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 5. Or, _things of nought_
> - <sup>b</sup> 9. Or, _in the beauty of holiness_
> - <sup>c</sup> 13. Or, _in his faithfulness_

## Chapter 97

### Jehovah’s power and dominion.

<sup>1</sup> Jehovah reigneth; let the earth rejoice;<br>Let the multitude of isles be glad.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Clouds and darkness are round about him:<br>Righteousness and justice are the foundation of his throne.<br>
<sup>3</sup> A fire goeth before him,<br>And burneth up his adversaries round about.<br>
<sup>4</sup> His lightnings lightened the world:<br>The earth saw, and trembled.<br>
<sup>5</sup> The mountains melted like wax at the presence of Jehovah,<br>At the presence of the Lord of the whole earth.<br>
<sup>6</sup> The heavens declare his righteousness,<br>And all the peoples have seen his glory.<br>
<sup>7</sup> Let all them be put to shame that serve graven images,<br>That boast themselves of idols:<br>Worship him, all ye gods.<br>
<sup>8</sup> Zion heard and was glad,<br>And the daughters of Judah rejoiced,<br>Because of thy judgments, O Jehovah.<br>
<sup>9</sup> For thou, Jehovah, art most high above all the earth:<br>Thou art exalted far above all gods.<br>
<sup>10</sup> O ye that love Jehovah, hate evil:<br>He preserveth the souls of his saints;<br>He delivereth them out of the hand of the wicked.<br>
<sup>11</sup> Light is sown for the righteous,<br>And gladness for the upright in heart.<br>
<sup>12</sup> Be glad in Jehovah, ye righteous;<br>And give thanks to his holy memorial _name_.<br>

## Chapter 98

### A call to praise Jehovah for his righteousness.

<sup>1</sup> > _A Psalm._Oh sing unto Jehovah a new song;<br>For he hath done marvellous things:<br>His right hand, and his holy arm, hath wrought salvation for him.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Jehovah hath made known his salvation:<br>His righteousness hath he openly showed in the sight of the nations.<br>
<sup>3</sup> He hath remembered his lovingkindness and his faithfulness toward the house of Israel:<br>All the ends of the earth have seen the salvation of our God.<br>
<sup>4</sup> Make a joyful noise unto Jehovah, all the earth:<br>Break forth and sing for joy, yea, sing praises.<br>
<sup>5</sup> Sing praises unto Jehovah with the harp;<br>With the harp and the voice of melody.<br>
<sup>6</sup> With trumpets and sound of cornet<br>Make a joyful noise before the King, Jehovah.<br>
<sup>7</sup> Let the sea roar, and the fulness thereof;<br>The world, and they that dwell therein;<br>
<sup>8</sup> Let the floods clap their hands;<br>Let the hills sing for joy together<br>
<sup>9</sup> Before Jehovah; for he cometh to judge the earth:<br>He will judge the world with righteousness,<br>And the peoples with equity.<br>

## Chapter 99

### Praise to Jehovah for his fidelity to Israel.

<sup>1</sup> Jehovah reigneth; let the peoples tremble:<br>He sitteth _above_ the cherubim; let the earth be m<sup>a</sup>oved.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Jehovah is great in Zion;<br>And he is high above all the peoples.<br>
<sup>3</sup> Let them praise thy great and terrible name:<br>Holy is he.<br>
<sup>4</sup> The king’s strength also loveth justice;<br>Thou dost establish equity;<br>Thou executest justice and righteousness in Jacob.<br>
<sup>5</sup> Exalt ye Jehovah our God,<br>And worship at his footstool:<br>Holy is he.<br>
<sup>6</sup> Moses and Aaron among his priests,<br>And Samuel among them that call upon his name;<br>They called upon Jehovah, and he answered them.<br>
<sup>7</sup> He spake unto them in the pillar of cloud:<br>They kept his testimonies,<br>And the statute that he gave them.<br>
<sup>8</sup> Thou answeredst them, O Jehovah our God:<br>Thou wast a God that forgavest them,<br>Though thou tookest vengeance of their doings.<br>
<sup>9</sup> Exalt ye Jehovah our God,<br>And worship at his holy hill;<br>For Jehovah our God is holy.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 1. Or, _is enthroned_

## Chapter 100

### All men exhorted to praise God.

<sup>1</sup> > _A Psalm of thanksgiving._Make a joyful noise unto Jehovah, all y<sup>a</sup>e lands.<sup>b</sup><br>
<sup>2</sup> Serve Jehovah with gladness:<br>Come before his presence with singing.<br>
<sup>3</sup> Know ye that Jehovah, he is God:<br>It is he that hath made us, <sup>c</sup>and we are his;<br>We are his people, and the sheep of his pasture.<br>
<sup>4</sup> Enter into his gates with <sup>d</sup>thanksgiving,<br>And into his courts with praise:<br>Give thanks unto him, and bless his name.<br>
<sup>5</sup> For Jehovah is good; his lovingkindness _endureth_ for ever,<br>And his faithfulness unto all generations.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 1. Or, _for the thank-offering_
> - <sup>b</sup> 1. Hebrew _all the earth_.
> - <sup>c</sup> 3. Another reading is, _and not we ourselves_.
> - <sup>d</sup> 4. Or, _a thank-offering_

## Chapter 101

### The psalmist’s profession of uprightness.

<sup>1</sup> > _A Psalm of David._I will sing of lovingkindness and justice:<br>Unto thee, O Jehovah, will I sing praises.<br>
<sup>2</sup> I will <sup>a</sup>behave myself wisely in a perfect way:<br>Oh when wilt thou come unto me?<br>I will walk within my house <sup>b</sup>with a perfect heart.<br>
<sup>3</sup> I will set no base thing before mine eyes:<br>I hate <sup>c</sup>the work of them that turn aside;<br>It shall not cleave unto me.<br>
<sup>4</sup> A perverse heart shall depart from me:<br>I will know no <sup>d</sup>evil thing.<br>
<sup>5</sup> Whoso privily slandereth his neighbor, him will I destroy:<br>Him that hath a high look and a proud heart will I not suffer.<br>
<sup>6</sup> Mine eyes shall be upon the faithful of the land, that they may dwell with me:<br>He that walketh in a perfect way, he shall minister unto me.<br>
<sup>7</sup> He that worketh deceit shall not dwell within my house:<br>He that speaketh falsehood shall not be established before mine eyes.<br>
<sup>8</sup> Morning by morning will I destroy all the wicked of the land;<br>To cut off all the workers of iniquity from the city of Jehovah.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 2. Or, _give heed unto the perfect way_
> - <sup>b</sup> 2. Or, _in the integrity of my heart_
> - <sup>c</sup> 3. Or, _the doing of unfaithfulness_
> - <sup>d</sup> 4. Or, _evil person_

## Chapter 102

### Prayer of an afflicted one for mercy on himself and on Zion.

<sup>1</sup> > _A Prayer of the afflicted, when he is overwhelmed, and poureth out his complaint before Jehovah._Hear my prayer, O Jehovah,<br>And let my cry come unto thee.<sup>a</sup><br>
<sup>2</sup> Hide not thy face from me in the day of my distress:<br>Incline thine ear unto me;<br>In the day when I call answer me speedily.<br>
<sup>3</sup> For my days consume away <sup>b</sup>like smoke,<br>And my bones are burned <sup>c</sup>as a firebrand.<br>
<sup>4</sup> My heart is smitten like grass, and withered;<br>For I forget to eat my bread.<br>
<sup>5</sup> By reason of the voice of my groaning<br>My bones cleave to my flesh.<br>
<sup>6</sup> I am like a pelican of the wilderness;<br>I am become as an owl of the waste places.<br>
<sup>7</sup> I watch, and am become like a sparrow<br>That is alone upon the house-top.<br>
<sup>8</sup> Mine enemies reproach me all the day;<br>They that are mad against me do curse by me.<br>
<sup>9</sup> For I have eaten ashes like bread,<br>And mingled my drink with weeping,<br>
<sup>10</sup> Because of thine indignation and thy wrath:<br>For thou hast taken me up, and cast me away.<br>
<sup>11</sup> My days are like a shadow that <sup>d</sup>declineth;<br>And I am withered like grass.<br>
<sup>12</sup> But thou, O Jehovah, <sup>e</sup>wilt abide for ever;<br>And thy memorial _name_ unto all generations.<br>
<sup>13</sup> Thou wilt arise, and have mercy upon Zion;<br>For it is time to have pity upon her,<br>Yea, the set time is come.<br>
<sup>14</sup> For thy servants take pleasure in her stones,<br>And have pity upon her dust.<br>
<sup>15</sup> So the nations shall fear the name of Jehovah,<br>And all the kings of the earth thy glory.<br>
<sup>16</sup> For Jehovah hath built up Zion;<br>He hath appeared in his glory.<br>
<sup>17</sup> He hath regarded the prayer of the destitute,<br>And hath not despised their prayer.<br>
<sup>18</sup> This shall be written for the generation to come;<br>And a people which shall be created shall praise <sup>f</sup>Jehovah.<br>
<sup>19</sup> For he hath looked down from the height of his sanctuary;<br>From heaven did Jehovah behold the earth;<br>
<sup>20</sup> To hear the sighing of the prisoner;<br>To loose <sup>g</sup>those that are appointed to death;<br>
<sup>21</sup> That men may declare the name of Jehovah in Zion,<br>And his praise in Jerusalem;<br>
<sup>22</sup> When the peoples are gathered together,<br>And the kingdoms, to serve Jehovah.<br>
<sup>23</sup> He <sup>h</sup>weakened my strength in the way;<br>He shortened my days.<br>
<sup>24</sup> I said, O my God, take me not away in the midst of my days:<br>Thy years are throughout all generations.<br>
<sup>25</sup> Of old didst thou lay the foundation of the earth;<br>And the heavens are the work of thy hands.<br>
<sup>26</sup> They shall perish, but thou shalt endure;<br>Yea, all of them shall wax old like a garment;<br>As a vesture shalt thou change them, and they shall be changed:<br>
<sup>27</sup> But thou art the same,<br>And thy years shall have no end.<br>
<sup>28</sup> The children of thy servants shall continue,<br>And their seed shall be established before thee.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 1. Or, _fainteth_
> - <sup>b</sup> 3. Or, _in smoke_
> - <sup>c</sup> 3. Or, _as a hearth_
> - <sup>d</sup> 11. Or, _is stretched out_
> - <sup>e</sup> 12. Or, _sittest_ as King
> - <sup>f</sup> 18. Hebrew _Jah_.
> - <sup>g</sup> 20. Hebrew _the children of death_.
> - <sup>h</sup> 23. Another reading is, _afflicted_ me with _his strength_

## Chapter 103

### Praise for Jehovah’s mercies.

<sup>1</sup> > __A Psalm_ of David._Bless Jehovah, O my soul;<br>And all that is within me, _bless_ his holy name.<br>
<sup>2</sup> Bless Jehovah, O my soul,<br>And forget not all his benefits:<br>
<sup>3</sup> Who forgiveth all thine iniquities;<br>Who healeth all thy diseases;<br>
<sup>4</sup> Who redeemeth thy life from <sup>a</sup>destruction;<br>Who crowneth thee with lovingkindness and tender mercies;<br>
<sup>5</sup> Who satisfieth <sup>b</sup>thy desire with good things,<br>_So that_ thy youth is renewed like the eagle.<br>
<sup>6</sup> Jehovah executeth righteous acts,<br>And judgments for all that are oppressed.<br>
<sup>7</sup> He made known his ways unto Moses,<br>His doings unto the children of Israel.<br>
<sup>8</sup> Jehovah is merciful and gracious,<br>Slow to anger, and abundant in lovingkindness.<br>
<sup>9</sup> He will not always chide;<br>Neither will he keep _his anger_ for ever.<br>
<sup>10</sup> He hath not dealt with us after our sins,<br>Nor rewarded us after our iniquities.<br>
<sup>11</sup> For as the heavens are high above the earth,<br>So great is his lovingkindness toward them that fear him.<br>
<sup>12</sup> As far as the east is from the west,<br>So far hath he removed our transgressions from us.<br>
<sup>13</sup> Like as a father pitieth his children,<br>So Jehovah pitieth them that fear him.<br>
<sup>14</sup> For he knoweth our frame;<br>He remembereth that we are dust.<br>
<sup>15</sup> As for man, his days are as grass;<br>As a flower of the field, so he flourisheth.<br>
<sup>16</sup> For the wind passeth over it, and it is gone;<br>And the place thereof shall know it no more.<br>
<sup>17</sup> But the lovingkindness of Jehovah is from everlasting to everlasting upon them that fear him,<br>And his righteousness unto children’s children;<br>
<sup>18</sup> To such as keep his covenant,<br>And to those that remember his precepts to do them.<br>
<sup>19</sup> Jehovah hath established his throne in the heavens;<br>And his kingdom ruleth over all.<br>
<sup>20</sup> Bless Jehovah, ye his angels,<br>That are mighty in strength, that fulfil his word,<br>Hearkening unto the voice of his word.<br>
<sup>21</sup> Bless Jehovah, all ye his hosts,<br>Ye ministers of his, that do his pleasure.<br>
<sup>22</sup> Bless Jehovah, all ye his works,<br>In all places of his dominion:<br>Bless Jehovah, O my soul.<br>

> - <sup>a</sup> 4. Or, _the pit_
> - <sup>b</sup> 5. Or, _thy years_. Or, _thy prime_. Hebrew _thine ornament_.

## Chapter 104

### Jehovah’s care over all his works.

<sup>1</sup> Bless Jehovah, O my soul.<br>O Jehovah my God, thou art very great;<br>Thou art clothed with honor and majesty:<br>
<sup>2</sup> Who coverest thyself with light as with a garment;<br>Who stretchest out the heavens like a curtain;<br>
<sup>3</sup> Who layeth the beams of his chambers in the waters;<br>Who maketh the clouds his chariot;<br>Who walketh upon the wings of the wind;<br>
<sup>4</sup> Who maketh <sup>a</sup>winds his messengers;<br>Flames of fire his ministers;<br>
<sup>5</sup> <sup>b</sup>Who laid the foundations of the earth,<br>That it should not be moved for ever.<br>
<sup>6</sup> Thou coveredst it with the deep as with a vesture;<br>The waters stood above the mountains.<br>
<sup>7</sup> At thy rebuke they fled;<br>At the voice of thy thunder they hasted away<br>
<sup>8</sup> <sup>c</sup>(The mountains rose, the valleys sank down)<br>Unto the place which thou hadst founded for them.<br>
<sup>9</sup> Thou hast set a bound that they may not pass over;<br>That they turn not again to cover the earth.<br>
<sup>10</sup> He sendeth forth springs into the valleys;<br>They run among the mountains;<br>
<sup>11</sup> They give drink to every beast of the field;<br>The wild asses quench their thirst.<br>
<sup>12</sup> By them the birds of the heavens have their habitation;<br>They <sup>d</sup>sing among the branches.<br>
<sup>13</sup> He watereth the mountains from his chambers:<br>The earth is filled with the fruit of thy works.<br>
<sup>14</sup> He causeth the grass to grow for the cattle,<br>And herb for the <sup>e</sup>service of man;<br>That he may bring forth <sup>f</sup>food out of the earth,<br>
<sup>15</sup> And wine that maketh glad the heart of man,<sup>g</sup><br>_And_ oil to make his face to shine,<br>And bread that strengtheneth man’s heart.<br>
<sup>16</sup> The trees of Jehovah are filled _with moisture_,<br>The cedars of Lebanon, which he hath planted;<br>
<sup>17</sup> Where the birds make their nests:<br>As for the stork, the fir-trees are her house.<br>
<sup>18</sup> The high mountains are for the wild goats;<br>The rocks are a refuge for the conies.<br>
